(51b)

where the respective kinetic parts of Maxwell fluctuations need to be identified from the terms
f_{1\mu\nu}f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
and
f_{2\mu\nu}f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
using the following relations

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}f_{I\mu\nu}f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.08821pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{{I}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=a_{I\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}-R^{\mu\nu}\right)a_{I\nu}+(D_{\mu}{a_{I}}^{\mu})^{2},\quad I=1,2.

(51c)

We continue with the objective of formulating the desired Laplace-type structure (
27
) for the differential operator that governs the quadratic STU fluctuations. To achieve this, we carried out a series of adjustments on the fluctuated action form (
51
) that are in the following order.

1.

The heat kernel treatment
2.3
necessitates that there be a distinct kinetic term corresponding to each off-shell degree of freedom or fluctuation present in the theory of interest. However, the quadratic action form (
51
) includes a few redundant components in the kinetic contributions of both the graviton and Maxwell fluctuations. To address this, we adopt the conventional practice of gauge-fixing the fluctuated theory by incorporating the gauge-fixing term,

\displaystyle\hskip-10.84006pt-\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\Big[\Big(D^{\mu}h_{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}D_{\rho}h^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\Big)\Big(D_{\nu}h^{\nu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}D^{\rho}h^{{{\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\Big((D^{\mu}a_{1\mu})^{2}+(D^{\mu}a_{2\mu})^{2}\Big)\Big],

(52)

where the graviton and Maxwell modes are accommodated through the choice of harmonic gauge
D^{\mu}h_{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}D_{\rho}h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}=0
and Lorentz gauge
D^{\mu}a^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}{\mu}}}=0,D^{\mu}a^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}{\mu}}}=0
, respectively. The gauge-fixing (
52
) is applicable to both the asymptotically flat and AdS cases, as the presence of the cosmological term always preserves the gauge invariance under the considered background field transformations. The introduction of this gauge-fixing procedure gives rise to an additional ghost term
Banerjee:2011oo
,

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\Big[2b_{1\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+R^{\mu\nu}\right)c_{1\nu}+2b_{2\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+R^{\mu\nu}\right)c_{2\nu}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+2b_{1}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}c_{1}+2b_{2}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}c_{2}-4b_{1}\bar{F}^{\rho\nu}D_{\rho}c_{1\nu}-4b_{2}\bar{F}^{\rho\nu}D_{\rho}c_{2\nu}\Big].

(53)

This term involves two sets of vector ghosts
(b_{1\mu},b_{2\mu},c_{1\mu},c_{2\mu})
and scalar ghosts
(b_{1},b_{2},c_{1},c_{2})
, arising respectively from the diffeomorphism and gauge invariance of the graviton and Maxwell fluctuations. This ghost term is minimal and remains non-interacting with the gauge-fixed component. Consequently, we have the flexibility to treat and evaluate the heat kernel contribution of the ghost part (
1
) separately.

2.

The preceding gauge-fixing procedure needs to be complemented by an appropriate treatment of the trace mode of the graviton for the remaining kinetic component
h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}
. Readers familiar with the literature (e.g., see
Christensen:1979iy
;
Christensen:1978md
;
Gibbons:1978ji
) may recognize the standard treatment of decomposing the graviton into its trace
h=h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}=\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}
and a symmetric traceless part
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}=h_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}h
. This decomposition enables to effectively treat the trace
h
and traceless
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
graviton components as distinct fluctuations that transform under the irreducible representations of
SL(2,\mathbb{C})
. As a consequence, the gauge-fixed kinetic portion of the graviton can be reformulated as

\displaystyle h_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}h^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}h^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}=\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}-\frac{1}{4}hD_{\rho}D^{\rho}h,

(54)

where the inner product between traceless graviton modes is defined by the following DeWitt metric,
11
11

11

In general, there exists a family of DeWitt metrics, parameterized by
\lambda_{\text{DW}}
(referred to as the DeWitt parameter), which takes the following form
DeWitt:1967gg

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}+\lambda_{\text{DW}}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\bar{g}^{\alpha\beta}\Big),

(55)

where the covariant or inverse operation is also represented by a similar relation. The specific relation (
56
), employed in this paper as well as in the works
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2022dfy
;
Castro:2018tg
;
Banerjee:2021pdy
, corresponds to the choice
\lambda_{\text{DW}}=-1/2
. However, interested readers are also referred to an alternative choice
\lambda_{\text{DW}}=-1
considered in
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Sen:2012qq
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Karan:2021teq
;
David:2021eoq
, where the approach does not involve decomposing the graviton fluctuation and treating its trace mode separately.

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\bar{g}^{\alpha\beta}\Big).

(56)

The operator
\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}
serves as an effective metric that projects onto the symmetric and traceless piece of the graviton, containing
10-1=9
off-shell degrees of freedom after gauge-fixing the STU theory. Consequently, in the subsequent heat kernel treatment of this study,
\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}
must be utilized as a projection operator to contract the pairs of indices for any matrix acting on
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
.
For instance, if
{M}
is an arbitrary matrix associated with the fluctuations of the traceless graviton component, i.e.,

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}{M}^{m}_{n}\tilde{\phi}^{n}=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}{{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta},

(57)

then the expression for
{M}^{2}
is given by,

\displaystyle{({M}^{2})}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}=\mathcal{G}_{\hat{h}_{\rho\sigma}\hat{h}_{\gamma\delta}}{{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\rho\sigma}}{{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\gamma\delta}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}.

(58)

Moreover, the trace operations on
M
and
M^{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{Tr}({M})=\mathcal{G}_{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}{{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}},\quad\text{Tr}({M}^{2})=\mathcal{G}_{\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}\hat{h}_{\rho\sigma}}\mathcal{G}_{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\gamma\delta}}{{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\rho\sigma}}{{M}}^{\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}\hat{h}_{\gamma\delta}}.\end{split}

(59)

3.

The revised kinetic contribution of the graviton, as presented in
eq.

54
, still requires further refinement. Notably, the graviton trace component involves a negative signature, treating
h
as a ghost field. This scenario highlights the familiar conformal factor problem in gravity, resulting in a divergent and ill-defined contribution to the Euclideanized one-loop path integral (
7
). To address this issue, we adopt the conventional procedure outlined by Gibbons
et al.

Gibbons:1978ac
;
Mazur:1989by
by implementing a conformal rotation along the imaginary axis with a new real graviton trace component
\hat{h}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\hat{h}=-\frac{i}{2}{h}.

(60)

It is important to note that the introduction of an additional
1/2
factor serves the purpose of aligning the kinetic contribution of the graviton trace with the same normalization state as that of the traceless component and Maxwell fluctuations (please refer to
eq.

62
).

4.

As a subsequent step, we need to modify the quadratic fluctuated STU action to ensure its self-adjoint properties in the desired operator form (
54
). This technical requirement necessitates adjusting the quadratic action to incorporate the Hermitian counterpart of each term. To achieve this, we identify appropriate terms with fluctuations
\tilde{\phi}_{m}=\big\{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},\hat{h},a_{1\mu},a_{2\mu},\tilde{\Phi}\big\}
and then extract their Hermitian partners using the following schematic approach involving arbitrary matrices
\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbb{K}^{\rho}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathbb{N}^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}+\tilde{\phi}_{m}(\mathbb{K}^{\rho})^{mn}D_{\rho}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\phi}_{m}\Big(\mathbb{N}^{mn}-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\rho}\mathbb{K}^{\rho})^{mn}+(\mathbb{K}^{\rho})^{mn}D_{\rho}\Big)\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\phi}_{n}\Big(\mathbb{N}^{mn}-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\rho}\mathbb{K}^{\rho})^{mn}-(\mathbb{K}^{\rho})^{mn}D_{\rho}\Big)\tilde{\phi}_{m}.

(61)

It is important to note that the above relations are derived by utilizing the commutative properties of the bosonic fluctuations and disregarding all the total derivative terms. This adjustment up to total derivatives is crucial since they lead to boundary terms that do not contribute to the integrations (
51
) around asymptotically flat and AdS backgrounds.

3.3.1
Gauge-fixed contribution

With incorporating the above rectifications, we finally arrive at the desired form of quadratic fluctuated STU theory (without including the ghost contribution (
1
)),

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\mathcal{S}[\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},\hat{h},a_{1\mu},a_{2\mu},\tilde{\Phi}]=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\tilde{\phi}^{n},

(62a)

where the simplified Laplace-type operator
\mathcal{H}
is expressed into the following components

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\tilde{\phi}^{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg\{\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}+2\Big(R^{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}R^{\nu\beta}-2\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\beta}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.54369pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.54369pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.19208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.70862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{2}\Big(\kappa_{1}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}+\kappa_{2}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\Big)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}+2\kappa_{1}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\dis \displaystyle+2\kappa_{2}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\left(D_{\rho}a_{2\alpha}\right)\bigg\}+a_{1\alpha}\bigg\{\bar{g}^{\alpha\beta}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}a_{1\beta}-{R}^{\alpha\beta}a_{1\beta}-\sqrt{2}\left(D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle-2\kappa_{1}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big(D_{\rho}\tilde{\Phi}\big)-2\sqrt{2}\Big(\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Big)(D_{\rho}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu})\bigg\}

\displaystyle+a_{2\alpha}\bigg\{\bar{g}^{\alpha\beta}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}a_{2\beta}-{R}^{\alpha\beta}a_{2\beta}-\sqrt{2}\left(D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle-2\kappa_{2}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\big(D_{\rho}\tilde{\Phi}\big)-2\sqrt{2}\Big(\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\rho}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Big)(D_{\rho}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu})\bigg\}.

(62b)

The above operator form represents different intricate interactions between the quadratic fluctuations of the STU model through the Einstein-Maxwell backgrounds. These interactions are noticed to be distributed among various non-minimal blocks that are determined by the background metric, curvature, and Maxwell strengths. To better organize these blocks, we have further restructured them in (
62
) to pair each quadratic term symmetrically by interchanging indices within each traceless graviton
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
and also with its quadratic counterpart (
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu},\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}
). During this process, one may make use of the background Einstein equation (
38
), the Maxwell equation (
39
), and the embedding condition (
3.2
) to facilitate necessary simplifications at appropriate steps. This adjustment is crucial to ensure correct trace results using the relations in (
59
). At this stage, we are well-prepared to conduct the heat kernel treatment as outlined in
section

2.3
, where we compare the Laplace-type operator
\mathcal{H}
for STU theory with the schematic representation in (
27
) and proceed to extract the required background heat kernel matrices.

The effective projection or identity matrices for individual off-shell STU fluctuations are read off as

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathcal{I}^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}+\hat{h}\hat{h}+a_{1\mu}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}a_{1\nu}+a_{2\mu}\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}a_{2\nu}+\tilde{\Phi}\tilde{\Phi},

(63)

Notice that the traceless graviton employs the DeWitt metric
\mathcal{G}^{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}
as an identity operator for the specific form given in
eq.

56
, whereas the same is replaced solely by the four-dimensional background metric
\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}
for the
U(1)
or Maxwell fluctuations. When we calculate the trace of (
63
), we find a total of nineteen off-shell degrees of freedom for the current gauged-fixed STU fluctuations
\{\tilde{\phi}_{m}\}
: nine stemming from the traceless graviton
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
, four from each Maxwell mode
a_{1\mu}
and
a_{2\mu}
, and one from each scalar field
\hat{h}
and
\tilde{\Phi}
, providing

\displaystyle\text{Tr}(\mathcal{I})=9+1+4+4+1=19.

(64)

Additionally, we derived the gauge-connection matrix
\omega^{\rho}
by analyzing the non-minimal (i.e., linear-derivative) terms in the operator form (
62
) as

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}(\omega^{\rho})^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\Big(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F_{1}}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F_{1}}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F_{1}}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F_{1}}^{\mu\rho}\Big)a_{1\alpha}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\Big(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F_{2}}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F_{2}}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F_{2}}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F_{2}}^{\mu\rho}\Big)a_{2\alpha}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}a_{1\alpha}\Big(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F_{1}}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F_{1}}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F_{1}}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F_{1}}^{\mu\rho}\Big)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}a_{2\alpha}\Big(\bar{g}^{\mu\rho}\bar{F_{2}}^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\rho}\bar{F_{2}}^{\mu\alpha}-\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{F_{2}}^{\nu\rho}-\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\bar{F_{2}}^{\mu\rho}\Big)\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{1}\Big(a_{1\alpha}\bar{F_{1}}^{\alpha\rho}\tilde{\Phi}-\tilde{\Phi}\bar{F_{1}}^{\alpha\rho}a_{1\alpha}\Big)+\kappa_{2}\Big(a_{2\alpha}\bar{F_{2}}^{\alpha\rho}\tilde{\Phi}-\tilde{\Phi}\bar{F_{2}}^{\alpha\rho}a_{2\alpha}\Big).

(65)

The relation above confirms the existence of only two primary sources of non-minimal couplings within the current quadratic fluctuation framework of the STU model. These couplings directly govern the interactions of two Maxwell fluctuations
\left(a_{1\alpha},a_{2\alpha}\right)
with the traceless graviton
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
and the dilaton
\tilde{\Phi}
. From a technical perspective, this interaction pattern gives rise to an indirect non-minimal coupling between
a_{1\alpha}
and
a_{2\alpha}
, subsequently leading to the emergence of relevant components within the
E
and
\Omega_{\rho\sigma}
matrices when derived using the relation (
3.3.1
) (for details, please refer to
appendix

A
). Furthermore, it is worth noting that the components of
\omega^{\rho}
are antisymmetric with respect to the relevant fluctuations. This particular characteristic is consistent with findings from previously reported similar heat kernel treatments, offering primary validation for the Laplace-type operator form derived in (
62
).

Similarly, we read off the matrix
P
from the derivative-free or minimal part of the operator form (
62
). The relevant components are expressed as follows

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}P^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\bigg(R^{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}+R^{\mu\beta\nu\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}R^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}R^{\mu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}R^{\nu\alpha}+\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}R^{\mu\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-2\Big(\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\beta}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.54369pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.54369pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.19208pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.70862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\beta}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.39673pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.39673pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.72404pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08861pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.20804pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.20804pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96788pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613p \displaystyle\quad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}{\rho}{\sigma}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}{\rho}{\sigma}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}-2\Lambda\Big)\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}^{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}^{\mu\beta}\bar{g}^{\nu\alpha}\right)\bigg)\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+2\hat{h}\Lambda\hat{h}-\tilde{\Phi}\Big({\kappa_{1}}^{2}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}{\mu}{\nu}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+{\kappa_{2}}^{2}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}{\mu}{\nu}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Big)\tilde{\Phi}-a_{1\mu}R^{\mu\nu}a_{1\nu}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-a_{2\mu}R^{\mu\nu}a_{2\nu}-2i\Big(\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}+\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}\Big)\Big(\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}+\hat{h}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big(D^{\mu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.20804pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.20804pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55505pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96788pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+D^{\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\alpha}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.06108pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.08702pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.34787pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\Big)\Big(\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}a_{2\alpha}+a_{2\alpha}\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\Big).

From here, our objective involves utilizing all the matrix-valued data recorded in
eqs.

63
,
3.3.1
and
3.3.1
to derive the components of the desired matrices

and

, which encode the complete details of all quadratic STU interactions in terms of the background metric and Maxwell fields. This process requires the formulation of additional terms that involve contraction and commutation operations between the connection
\omega_{\rho}
and the covariant derivative
D_{\rho}
, as delineated by the formulas in
eqs.

30
,
32
and
31

\text{Tr}\left(E\right)
,
\text{Tr}\left(E^{2}\right)
, and
\text{Tr}\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)
over all relevant components. Although these calculations are highly intricate and laborious, they may provide valuable insights to interested readers. We have provided a systematic outline of the underlying steps in
appendix

\displaystyle=-12\Lambda+7\left(\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}{\mu\nu}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}{\mu\nu}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right),

t][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\Lambda\Bigg(60+\frac{8\kappa_{1}^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}\Bigg)\left(\bar{F}_{1\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}+\bar{F}_{2\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right).

(69)

The aforementioned trace relations are simplified by incorporating appropriate on-shell identities (see
section

A.1
), expressing them solely in terms of distinct irreducible invariants of Einstein-Maxwell backgrounds for the two
U(1)^{2}
-charged STU models with
\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2}=-1
. However, it has been observed that nearly all invariants involving background Maxwell field strengths in the specific trace data are precisely canceled out within the
a_{4}(x)
formula (
33
). The final Seeley-DeWitt result involves only
\Lambda\bar{F}_{1\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
and
\Lambda\bar{F}_{2\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}
, specifically for the case
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})
in the STU truncation. These can be collectively encoded inside the effective invariant
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
, where
R=4\Lambda
, in accordance with the choice of the embedding condition (
3.2
). All of this leads to the following result

\displaystyle(4\pi)^{2}{a_{4}}^{\text{gauge-fixed}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{169}{180}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}+\Bigg(\frac{311}{180}-\frac{2\kappa_{1}^{2}}{3\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}\Bigg)R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\Bigg(\frac{4}{9}-\frac{\kappa_{1}^{2}}{6\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}\Bigg)R^{2}+\frac{\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}-1\right)^{2}}{6\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu},

(70)

where we have set
\chi=1
for the graviton and
U(1)
or Maxwell fluctuations. It is worth noting some insightful remarks about the Seeley-DeWitt result (
3.3.1
). The coefficient of
R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
remains robust for a theory with fixed off-shell degrees of freedom. In contrast, the specific coefficients for
R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}
,
R^{2}
and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
are not fundamental but rather sensitive to the type of non-minimal couplings and gauge interactions that are effective between fluctuations. Moreover, their respective contributions can be interchanged through the one-shell Einstein equation (
3.2
). Therefore, at any stage, it is never advisable to disregard the background invariants proportional to
\bar{F}_{1\mu\nu}
and
\bar{F}_{2\mu\nu}
, which were present in the trace data obtained in
eqs.

67
,
68
and
69
but canceled out inside the final result (
3.3.1
).

3.3.2
Ghost contribution

Now, we need to proceed with a similar heat kernel computation for the ghost term (
1
), which further refines the gauge-fixed contribution (
3.3.1
) of the Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
a_{4}(x)
. To begin, we can express the ghost action as

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\Big[2b_{I\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+R^{\mu\nu}\right)c_{J\nu}\delta_{IJ}+2b_{I}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}c_{J}\delta_{IJ}-4b_{I}\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\nu}D_{\rho}c_{K\nu}\delta_{IJK}\Big],

(71)

where
\delta_{IJ}
and
\delta_{IJK}
are the Kronecker deltas with indices
I,J,K=1,2
enumerating all the ghost fields correspond to the two
U(1)
gauge or Maxwell species. Clearly, the kinetic terms need to be diagonalized to make use of the Laplace-type quadratic operator for the heat kernel methodology. A convenient approach is to introduce the following redefinitions between the ghost species

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}b_{I\mu}\to\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(c_{I\mu}-ib_{I\mu}\right),\quad c_{I\mu}\to\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(c_{I\mu}+ib_{I\mu}\right),\\
b_{I}\to\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(c_{I}-ib_{I}\right),\quad c_{I}\to\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(c_{I}+ib_{I}\right).\end{gathered}

This allows us to extract the desired form of the kinetic operator (up to quadratic order) operating on the six ghost fluctuations
\tilde{\phi}_{m}=\big\{b_{I\mu},c_{I\mu},b_{I},c_{I}\big\}
as

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\mathcal{S}_{\text{ghost}}=\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\tilde{\phi}^{n},

(74a)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathcal{H}^{m}_{n}\tilde{\phi}^{n}

\displaystyle=b_{I\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+R^{\mu\nu}\right)b_{J\nu}\delta_{IJ}+c_{I\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}+R^{\mu\nu}\right)c_{J\nu}\delta_{IJ}

\displaystyle\quad+b_{I}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}b_{J}\delta_{IJ}+c_{I}D_{\rho}D^{\rho}c_{J}\delta_{IJ}+b_{I\mu}\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}b_{K}+iD_{\rho}c_{K}\right)\delta_{IJK}

\displaystyle\quad+c_{I\mu}\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}c_{K}-iD_{\rho}b_{K}\right)\delta_{IJK}-b_{I}\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}b_{K\mu}-iD_{\rho}c_{K\mu}\right)\delta_{IJK}

\displaystyle\quad-c_{I}\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\left(D_{\rho}c_{K\mu}+iD_{\rho}b_{K\mu}\right)\delta_{IJK}.

(74b)

From here, the task is to write the forms of the following necessary heat kernel matrices (as defined in
section

2.3
)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}\mathcal{I}^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=b_{I\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\right)b_{J\nu}+c_{I\mu}\left(\bar{g}^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\right)c_{J\nu}+b_{I}\left(\delta_{IJ}\right)b_{J}+c_{I}\left(\delta_{IJ}\right)c_{J},

(75)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}P^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=b_{I\mu}\left(R^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\right)b_{J\nu}+c_{I\mu}\left(R^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\right)c_{J\nu},

(76)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}(\omega^{\rho})^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}b_{I\mu}\left(\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K}-\frac{1}{2}b_{I}\left(\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K\mu}+\frac{1}{2}b_{I\mu}\left(i\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt-\frac{1}{2}c_{I}\left(i\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K\mu}+\frac{1}{2}c_{I\mu}\left(\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K}-\frac{1}{2}c_{I}\left(\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K\mu}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{1}{2}c_{I\mu}\left(i\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K}-\frac{1}{2}b_{I}\left(i\bar{F}_{J}^{\rho\mu}\right)c_{K\mu},

(77)

which further provides,

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}E^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=b_{I\mu}\left(R^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\right)b_{J\nu}+c_{I\mu}\left(R^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\right)c_{J\nu},

(78)

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{m}\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\right)^{mn}\tilde{\phi}_{n}

\displaystyle=b_{I\mu}\left(R^{{{\mu}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\sigma}}}\delta_{IJ}\right)b_{J\nu}+c_{I\mu}\left(R^{{{\mu}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}{\sigma}}}\delta_{IJ}\right)c_{J\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}b_{I\mu}\left(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K}+\frac{1}{2}b_{I}\left(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K\mu}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}b_{I\mu}\left(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K}+\frac{1}{2}c_{I}\left(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}c_{I\mu}\left(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)b_{K}-\frac{1}{2}b_{I}\left(iD^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K\mu}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}c_{I\mu}\left(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K}+\frac{1}{2}c_{I}\left(D^{\mu}\bar{F}_{J\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJK}\right)c_{K\mu}.

(79)

It is essential to recognize that all other matrix components that are technically valid but nullified by the application of the equations of motion (
38
) to (
40
) for our specific choice of EM background embedded STU supergravity model. Taking into account the derived components for the matrices
\mathcal{I}
,
E
and
\Omega_{\rho\sigma}
, we can determine the following traces for the ghosts

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{Tr}(\mathcal{I})&=4\delta_{II}+4\delta_{II}+\delta_{II}+\delta_{II}=20,\\
\text{Tr}(E)&=R\delta_{II}+R\delta_{II}=16\Lambda,\\
\text{Tr}(E^{2})&=2R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}\delta_{IJ}\delta_{JI}=4R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu},\\
\text{Tr}\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)&=-2R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\delta_{IJ}\delta_{JI}=-4R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}.\end{split}

(80)

This trace data is sufficient to determine the ghost contribution to the third-order Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
a_{4}(x)
,

\displaystyle(4\pi)^{2}{a_{4}}^{\text{ghost}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{9}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-\frac{17}{9}R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}-\frac{17}{18}R^{2}.

(81)

One must note that this result incorporates
\chi=-1
in the formula (
33
) due to the inclusion of bosonic ghosts where the negative signature arises as the ghosts follow a reverse spin-statistics compared to the physical fields.

3.4
Results

In gravitational theories with quantum fluctuations, the logarithmic correction to black hole entropy induced by the one-loop effective action is well-known to be associated with trace anomalies (e.g., see
Keeler:2014nn
;
Xiao:2021zly
;
Aros:2013taa
). Within the framework of effective action formulated by the heat kernel expansion and the background field formalism (as detailed in
section

2
), the trace anomalies explicitly appear as terms within the heat expansion coefficients and depend on the background curvature invariants. Specifically, the trace anomalies are encoded within the third-order Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
a_{4}(x)
for four-dimensional theories.

Our current objective is to unveil trace anomalies within
a_{4}(x)
for the ongoing analysis of the quantized STU supergravity model around Einstein-Maxwell-AdS backgrounds. Specifically, we will demonstrate that the square of the Weyl tensor
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and the four-dimensional Euler-Gauss-Bonnet density
E_{4}
respectively appear as the type-B and type-A trace anomalies, defined as

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}&=R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-2R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{3}R^{2},\\
E_{4}&=R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-4R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}+R^{2}.\end{split}

(82)

We need to combine the computed gauge-fixed contribution (
3.3.1
) and ghost contribution (
81
). These contributions are computed separately since the ghosts do not interact with the gauge-fixed sector of the theory containing physical modes. The combination of gauge-fixed and ghost parts is straightforward, resulting in

\displaystyle(4\pi)^{2}{a_{4}}^{\text{STU}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{209}{180}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-\Bigg(\frac{29}{180}+\frac{2\kappa_{1}^{2}}{3\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}\Bigg)R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad-\Bigg(\frac{25}{18}-\frac{\kappa_{1}^{2}}{6\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}\Bigg)R^{2}+\frac{\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}-1\right)^{2}}{6\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}.

(83)

For the solution of the STU supergravity model embedded with Einstein-Maxwell backgrounds in AdS space (or with a negative cosmological constant
\Lambda
), the Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
a_{4}(x)
can be decomposed in terms of a sum of four-derivative background terms, including the trace anomalies:

\displaystyle(4\pi)^{2}{a_{4}}^{\text{STU}}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{c}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-\mathfrak{a}E_{4}+\mathfrak{b}_{1}R^{2}+\mathfrak{b}_{2}R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu},

(84a)

where the coefficients
\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathfrak{a}
,
\mathfrak{b}_{1}
, and
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
can be extracted from the relation (
3.4
), derived using the two-derivative action of the present theory. The coefficients
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{a}
, respectively multiplying
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and
E_{4}
, are recognized as the two central charges of the conformal anomaly in 4D. The results of the coefficients in (
84a
) are summarized as follows

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{269}{40}-\frac{\kappa_{1}^{2}}{\kappa_{1}^{4}+1}\right),

(84b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{389}{120}-\frac{\kappa_{1}^{2}}{\kappa_{1}^{4}+1}\right),

(84c)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{18}\left(19+\frac{\kappa_{1}^{2}}{\kappa_{1}^{4}+1}\right),

(84d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\kappa_{1}^{2}-1\right)^{2}}{6\left(\kappa_{1}^{4}+1\right)}.

(84e)

It is important to note that the absence of the dilaton coupling constant
\kappa_{2}
from the results presented in
sections

3.4
,
84
and
3.3.1
should not raise concerns. The
\kappa_{2}
contributions are consistently taken into account in all relevant places. We substituted them using the specific choice
\kappa_{2}=-1/\kappa_{1}
in order to simplify the forms of the heat kernel results computed for the two truncation cases of the STU model, i.e., when
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)
and
(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})
(refer to
section

3.2
). While it is feasible to rewrite the results in terms of relatively complex forms involving both
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
at any point, such a task is not straightforward and requires the use of trace data and identities provided in
sections

A.3
and
A.1
.

The derived trace anomaly data (
84
) is specific to the chosen theory, relying entirely on the one-loop fluctuations and embedded asymptotically-AdS EM background in the STU supergravity model. Equivalent results for the
U(1)^{2}
-charged STU model in asymptotically-flat EM backgrounds can be easily obtained by setting the cosmological constant to zero,
\Lambda=0
. In this flat space limit, terms such as
R^{2}
and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
vanish, and the relation (
3.4
) for
a_{4}(x)
is solely governed by the Weyl anomaly
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and the Euler density
E_{4}
, with non-vanishing coefficients
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{a}
. Moreover, the
a_{4}(x)
coefficient in the flat-space limit becomes invariant under electromagnetic duality, as it is independent of terms proportional to the effective field strength
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}
(as defined in
section

3.2
). Interestingly, this property persists even when transitioning beyond the flat limit and considering AdS backgrounds embedded in the
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)
case of the STU theory, resulting in a vanishing
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
. However, the STU model with
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})
breaks the electromagnetic duality invariant nature of
a_{4}(x)
due to the induction of a non-zero
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
.

Before delving into the implications of the heat kernel and trace anomaly data for computing the logarithmic correction to the entropy of embedded black holes (refer to
section

4
), it is essential to assess their consistency with existing literature. While the current STU supergravity model stands as the most natural and non-trivial generalization of previously explored bosonic supergravity models (for example, refer to the review
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Karan:2021teq
;
Sen:2013ns
;
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Karan:2020sk
;
David:2021eoq
;
Sen:2012qq
;
Keeler:2014nn
;
Castro:2018tg
;
Karan:2022dfy
), there is no direct path leading to these available results by setting appropriate limits in the relations presented in
sections

3.4
and
84
. Technically, this is because the dilaton-coupling-independent terms in the heat kernel or trace anomaly data are sensitive to the specific off-shell degrees of freedom and their non-minimal interactions within the theory, making them less controllable. Nevertheless, we have conducted meticulous checks, starting with the original form of trace data recorded in
section

A.3
and proceeding with top-down manipulations. This approach has yielded exact results for the fluctuated Einstein-Maxwell theory
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Karan:2021teq
;
Sen:2013ns
or the boson sector of
\mathcal{N}=2
supergravity (for
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=0
)
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Karan:2020sk
;
David:2021eoq
;
Sen:2012qq
;
Keeler:2014nn
, the Kaluza-Klein system (for
\kappa_{1}=\sqrt{3},\kappa_{2}=0,\Lambda=0
)
Castro:2018tg
, and Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton models (for
\kappa_{1}=1,\sqrt{3},1/\sqrt{3}
and
\kappa_{2}=0
)
Karan:2022dfy
. Each of these emerges as a distinct and consistent limiting case of the current STU model (
37
) heat kernel results obtained in
sections

3.4
and
84
.

4
Logarithmic corrections to AdS
4
and flat
4
black hole entropy

In this section, we delve into the derivation of the logarithmic correction to the entropy of black holes embedded in the truncated four-dimensional STU supergravity models discussed in
section

2
. These backgrounds encompass all the Kerr-Newman-AdS, Reissner-Nordström-AdS, Kerr-AdS, and Schwarzschild-AdS black holes, along with the Kerr-Newman, Reissner-Nordström, Kerr, and Schwarzschild black holes, which are generic solutions of the Einstein-Maxwell theory evolving with and without a negative cosmological constant, respectively. Here, we interpret or uplift them as the background solutions of the
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD-AdS and EMD theories intersecting with the gauged and ungauged STU supergravities in four spacetime dimensions.

At this stage, we apply the framework established in
section

2
, which necessitates the utilization of the Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
a_{4}(x)
calculated in
sections

3.3
and
3.4
. Specifically, our current objective is to provide the trace anomaly form (
84
) of
a_{4}(x)
as a precursor for determining the logarithmic corrections for both non-extremal and extremal black holes embedded in the STU models. In the local contribution
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
, all the four-derivative background invariants, including the
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and
E_{4}
anomalies, need to be integrated over the appropriate part of the geometry of the concerned black hole backgrounds. This leads us to the following general formula for obtaining the local part of logarithmic entropy corrections

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathfrak{c}}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\bar{g}}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\bar{g}}E_{4}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt+\frac{\mathfrak{b}_{1}}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\bar{g}}R^{2}+\frac{\mathfrak{b}_{2}}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\bar{g}}R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu},

(85)

where the coefficients
\mathfrak{a}
,
\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathfrak{b}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
do not affect the integrations since their values, as obtained in
eq.

84b
, are constants fixed by the choice of theory. For non-extremal or finite-temperature AdS
4
and flat
4
black holes, the integrations need to be performed over the entire background geometry
\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}
. Meanwhile, for extremal black holes, we observe that the contributions to
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
remain the same whether the integration (
4
) is executed over the full geometry or only the finite piece of near-horizon geometry.

In the following subsections, we systematically derive specific relations for computing contributions to
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
in STU models. These relations are expressed in terms of various dimensionless ratios involving different black hole parameters and the anomaly coefficients
\mathfrak{a}
,
\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathfrak{b}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{b}_{2}
. Throughout this process, our primary focus is on evaluating the curvature invariant integrations, as detailed in relation (
4
), for the most generic non-extremal background of a four-dimensional Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole. Subsequently, we emphasize on the extremal and near-horizon cases, along with their asymptotically-flat limits. Furthermore, the same analysis is extended to other AdS
4
and flat
4
counterparts of Reissner-Nordström, Kerr, and Schwarzschild black holes. As a final output, we present the explicit results for the logarithmic correction to the entropy of all black holes embedded in both the gauged and ungauged versions of the STU supergravity theory.

4.1
Black hole backgrounds in STU supergravity and
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions

4.1.1
Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
black hole

We start with a four-dimensional non-extremal Kerr-Newman-AdS (KNAdS) black hole, representing the most interesting and generic Einstein-Maxwell (EM) background in our chosen STU models. In terms of the standard Boyer-Lindquist type coordinates, the metric for this charged and rotating background is given by
Caldarelli:1999x

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta_{r}}{\rho^{2}}\left(\mathrm{d}t-\frac{a\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}\mathrm{d}r^{2}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}{\rho^{2}}\left(a\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2},

(86)

where we set
G_{N}=1
. The parameters
\Delta_{r}
,
\Delta_{\theta}
,
\rho
, and
\Xi
are related to the physical mass
M
, angular momentum
J
, and electric charge
Q
of the black hole, as well as the boundary radius
\ell
of AdS
4
space, via:

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}M=\frac{m}{\Xi^{2}},\kern 5.0ptJ=\frac{ma}{\Xi^{2}},\kern 5.0ptQ=\frac{q}{\Xi},\kern 5.0pt\Xi=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}},\\[5.0pt]
\Delta_{r}=(r^{2}+a^{2})\left(1+\frac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)-2mr+q^{2},\\
\Delta_{\theta}=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta,\kern 5.0pt\rho^{2}=r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta.\end{gathered}

It is important to note that this background setup is valid only when the rotational parameter
a
satisfies
a^{2}<\ell^{2}
and becomes singular in the limit
a^{2}=\ell^{2}
. Throughout our analysis, we consider
a\geq 0
, and obtain all interested results by replacing
a\to\left|a\right|
everywhere without any loss of generality. Furthermore, we consistently turn off terms related to the magnetic charge parameters in order to embed the charged EM backgrounds into the current STU supergravity models. The metric (
4.1.1
) satisfies the background EM field equations (
3.2
) for
R=4\Lambda=-\dfrac{12}{\ell^{2}}
with an electrically-charged vector potential
\bar{A}_{\mu}
and an associated Maxwell field strength tensor
\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}
, given respectively by

\displaystyle\bar{A}=-\frac{qr}{\rho^{2}}\left(\mathrm{d}t-\frac{a\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right),

(90)

and

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=-\frac{2q^{2}}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{4}}\left(r^{4}-6a^{2}r^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\right).

(91)

As per the specific embedding choices (
3.2
) and (
3.2
), the KNAdS background is interpreted as a solution of the truncated STU models (
37
) for an effective electric charge parameter,

\displaystyle q=\sqrt{q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}}\quad\text{or}\kern 5.0ptQ=\sqrt{Q_{1}^{2}+Q_{2}^{2}},

(92)

where
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
represent the respective charge parameters of the two
U(1)
gauge fields, satisfying (
90
) and (
91
). If the outer event horizon of the KNAdS black hole (
4.1.1
) is located at
r=r_{+}
, then one obtains
r_{+}
by finding the largest real root of
g_{00}=\Delta_{r}=0
and fixing the mass parameter as

\displaystyle m=\frac{r_{+}}{2}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{a^{2}+q^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right).

(93)

At this level, it is standard practice to consider the analytical continuation of the Lorentzian metric (
4.1.1
) by
t\to i\tau
and
a\to ia
for obtaining the Euclidean structure of the KNAdS black hole. The associated Bekenstein-Hawking entropy is given by

\displaystyle S_{\text{BH}}=4\pi\frac{\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)}.

(94)

Also, the regularity at
r=r_{+}
requires identifying
\tau\sim\tau+\beta
where inverse of
\beta
is a measure of the Hawking temperature of the black hole,

\displaystyle T_{\text{bh}}=\beta^{-1}=\frac{r_{+}}{4\pi\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{3r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+q^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right).

(95)

With the above setup, the four curvature invariants necessary for (
4
) in the electrically-charged and rotating KNAdS black hole case are expressed as follows

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{48}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{6}}\bigg[8r^{4}\left(q^{2}-2mr\right)^{2}-m^{2}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad-8r^{2}\left(q^{2}-3mr\right)\left(q^{2}-2mr\right)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(q^{4}-10mrq^{2}+18m^{2}r^{2}\Big)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{2}\bigg],

(96a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24}{\ell^{4}}+\frac{8}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{6}}\bigg[48r^{4}\left(q^{2}-2mr\right)^{2}-6m^{2}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad-48r^{2}\left(q^{2}-3mr\right)\left(q^{2}-2mr\right)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(5q^{4}-60mrq^{2}+108m^{2}r^{2}\Big)\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{2}\bigg],

(96b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},

(96c)

\displaystyle R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{24q^{2}}{\ell^{2}\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{4}}\left(r^{4}-6a^{2}r^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\right).

(96d)

Next, we proceed to integrate all the aforementioned invariants over the Euclideanized KNAdS
4
black hole geometry. However, these integrations pose challenges due to divergence caused by the infinite volume of AdS
4
. In this paper, we address this issue by employing holographic renormalization principles
Skenderis:2002wp
. Specifically, we introduce a cutoff at
r=r_{c}
on the boundary of the KNAdS
4
geometry (
4.1.1
). Following this, we introduce a holographic counterterm,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}=\int_{\partial(\text{AdS${}_{4}$})}\mathrm{d}^{3}y\sqrt{\det\gamma}\left(c_{1}+c_{2}\mathscr{R}\right).

(97)

Here,
\mathscr{R}
represents the Ricci scalar associated with the metric
\gamma_{\mu\nu}
characterizing the KNAdS
4
boundary geometry,

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}y^{\mu}\mathrm{d}y^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Delta_{r_{c}}}{\rho_{c}^{2}}\left(i\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{a\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2}+\frac{\rho_{c}^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}+\frac{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}{\rho_{c}^{2}}\left(ia\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\frac{r_{c}^{2}+a^{2}}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\phi\right)^{2},

(98a)

where

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Delta_{r_{c}}=(r_{c}^{2}+a^{2})\left(1+\frac{r_{c}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)-2m{r_{c}}+q^{2},\quad{\rho_{c}}^{2}=r_{c}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta.\end{gathered}

For this boundary geometry, we determine

\displaystyle\det\gamma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Xi^{2}}\left[q^{2}-2mr_{c}+\left(r_{c}^{2}+a^{2}\right)\left(1+\frac{r_{c}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\right]\rho_{c}^{2}\sin^{2}\theta,

(99a)

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\rho_{c}^{6}}\bigg[r_{c}^{4}+\left(r_{c}^{2}-2m{r_{c}}+q^{2}\right)a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\Big(r_{c}^{2}\left(1-5\cos^{2}\theta\right)-3a^{2}\cos^{4}\theta\Big)\rho_{c}^{2}\bigg],

(99b)

and derive the following form of the holographic counterterm (
97
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}=\frac{4\pi\beta}{\Xi\ell}\left[c_{1}r_{c}^{3}+\frac{1}{6\ell^{2}}\Big(c_{1}\left(4a^{2}+3\ell^{2}\right)\ell^{2}+4c_{2}\left(3\ell^{2}-2a^{2}\right)\Big)r_{c}-c_{1}m\ell^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(r_{c}^{-1}\right).

(100)

The integration range is defined as
0\leq\tau\leq\beta
,
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
, and
0\leq\phi\leq 2\pi
. Subsequently, the boundary term (
100
) needs to be combined with the bulk
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
term (
4
), involving integrations over the KNAdS
4
background invariants. The resulting bulk-boundary combinations contain
\mathcal{O}\left(r_{c}^{-1}\right)
order terms that vanish as
r_{c}\to\infty
. The next step involves appropriately setting the coefficients
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
to cancel the
r_{c}^{3}
and
r_{c}
divergences. This procedure yields a finite contribution of all the integrated background invariants, leading to a regularized
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
given by,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}=\lim_{r_{c}\to\infty}\left[\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}\mathrm{d}\tau\int_{r_{+}}^{r_{c}}\mathrm{d}r\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathrm{d}\theta\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\,\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,{a_{4}}(\tau,r,\theta,\phi)+\mathcal{C}_{\text{HCT}}\right].

(101)

Here, the Seeley-DeWitt coefficient
{a_{4}}(\tau,r,\theta,\phi)
appears as a function of the invariants
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
,
R^{2}
, and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
around the Euclideanized KNAdS
4
geometry (
4.1.1
). The integrated invariants, as required in (
4
), take the following particular forms,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=W_{1}+\beta W_{2}+\frac{W_{3}}{\beta},

(102a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(102b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}

\displaystyle=R_{1}+\beta R_{2},

(102c)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=F_{1}+\beta F_{2},

(102d)

where the pieces
W_{i}
,
R_{i}
, and
F_{i}
are isolated based on their dependency on the inverse temperature parameter
\beta
. In particular, they are expressed as

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)}{2\Xi a^{5}\ell^{2}r_{+}^{3}}\bigg[\Big(3a^{4}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)+4a^{2}\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}-3\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+}^{4}\Big)ar_{+}

\displaystyle\quad-3\left(r_{+}^{4}-a^{4}\right)\Big(a^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)-\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+}^{2}\Big)\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg],

(102e)

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{16\pi\Xi a^{5}\ell^{4}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)r_{+}^{4}}\bigg[3a^{9}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}r_{+}-4a^{7}\left(3r_{+}^{4}+12\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{4}\right)r_{+}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+2a^{5}\left(5r_{+}^{4}-14\ell^{2}r_{+}^{2}+\ell^{4}\right)r_{+}^{5}-4a^{3}\left(\ell^{4}-9r_{+}^{4}\right)r_{+}^{7}+3a\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)^{2}r_{+}^{9}

\displaystyle\quad-3\left(r_{+}^{4}-a^{4}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)\Big(a^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{+}^{2}\right)-\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)r_{+}^{2}\Big)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg],

(102f)

\displaystyle W_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)}{\Xi a^{5}r_{+}^{2}}\bigg[ar_{+}\left(3a^{4}+2a^{2}r_{+}^{2}+3r_{+}^{4}\right)-3\left(r_{+}^{4}-a^{4}\right)\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg],

(102g)

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{24}{\Xi\ell^{2}}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right),\quad R_{2}=\frac{12r_{+}}{\pi\Xi\ell^{4}}\left(\ell^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right),

(102h)

\displaystyle F_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{24r_{+}^{2}}{\Xi\ell^{2}},\quad F_{2}=\frac{6r_{+}}{\pi\Xi\ell^{4}\left(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}\right)}\left[3r_{+}^{4}+\left(\ell^{2}+a^{2}\right)r_{+}^{2}-a^{2}\ell^{2}\right].

(102i)

The regularization procedure outlined above is applicable to all background geometries considered in this paper. The holographic renormalization prescription employed is natural, producing consistent and unambiguous results by isolating the finite piece of the bulk effective action from divergent terms. In contrast, any naive regularization treatment that merely removes the divergent term would fail to yield physically sensible results, as demonstrated in
eqs.

101
and
102
. As an illustrative example, consider verifying the Euler characteristic for AdS
4
spacetime obtained through this holographic renormalization procedure:

\displaystyle\chi=\lim_{r_{c}\to\infty}\left[\frac{1}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{\text{KNAdS${}_{4}$}}\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}+\frac{1}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{\partial(\text{KNAdS${}_{4}$})}\mathrm{d}^{3}y\sqrt{\det\gamma}\left(c_{1}+c_{2}\mathscr{R}\right)\right]=2,

(103)

where
c_{1}=-\frac{8}{\ell^{3}}
and
c_{2}=\frac{2}{\ell}
. Remarkably, this result aligns precisely with the prediction from the integrated four-dimensional Euler density
E_{4}
using the Gauss-Bonnet-Chern theorem
Chern:1945wp
. This alignment validates the current renormalization procedure, and for more discussions, we refer the reader to
David:2021eoq
;
Karan:2022dfy
.

Finally, the integration of the invariants (
102
) culminates in the ultimate expression for
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
for the non-extremal Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
black hole in STU models,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(KN-AdS)}}=\frac{\mathfrak{c}W_{3}}{\beta}-\left(4\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{c}W_{1}-\mathfrak{b}_{1}R_{1}-\mathfrak{b}_{2}F_{1}\right)+\beta\left(\mathfrak{c}W_{2}+\mathfrak{b}_{1}R_{2}+\mathfrak{b}_{2}F_{2}\right),

(104)

where the non-topological terms
W_{i}
,
R_{i}
and
F_{i}
are expressed in terms of different black hole parameters, as detailed in
eq.

102
. Notably, the relation (
104
) precisely aligns with the results obtained in
David:2021eoq
for vanishing magnetic charges on the KNAdS
4
background. Furthermore, we verify the consistency of the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions derived for the other black hole backgrounds considered in this paper as distinct special cases.

4.1.2
Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
black hole

The background metric for a 4D non-extremal Reissner-Nordström-AdS (RNAdS
4
) black hole solution of the STU field equations (
38
) is expressed as follows

\displaystyle\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-f(r)\mathrm{d}t^{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}r^{2}}{f(r)}+r^{2}\left(\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}\right),\kern 5.0pt\bar{A}=\bar{A}_{t}(r)\mathrm{d}t=-\frac{q}{r}\mathrm{d}t,

(105a)

where the function
f(r)
is expressed in terms of black hole mass parameter
m
, effective electric charge
q
(refer to (
92
)) and AdS
4
radius
\ell
,

\displaystyle f(r)=1-\frac{2m}{r}+\frac{q^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}}.

(105b)

Upon Euclidean continuation (
t\to i\tau
), the corresponding Hawking temperature is given by

\displaystyle T_{\text{bh}}=\beta^{-1}=\frac{1}{4\pi r_{+}}\left(1-\frac{q^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{3r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(106)

where
r_{+}
represents the outer horizon radius at which
g_{00}(r_{+})=f(r_{+})=0
. This yields the mass parameter as

\displaystyle m=\frac{r_{+}}{2}\left(1+\frac{q^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right).

(107)

For the Euclideanized background geometry (
105
), the required invariants are given by

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{48}{r^{8}}\left(mr-q^{2}\right)^{2},

(108a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24}{\ell^{4}}+\frac{8}{r^{8}}\left(6m^{2}r^{2}-12mq^{2}r+5q^{4}\right),

(108b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},

(108c)

\displaystyle R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{24q^{2}}{\ell^{2}r^{4}}.

(108d)

We then employ the holographic renormalization procedure as outlined in
section

4.1.1
to derive regulated values for the integration of the above invariants on the RNAdS
4
. The results are as follows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\beta r_{+}^{3}}{5\pi\ell^{4}}\left(4+\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{\ell^{4}}{r_{+}^{4}}\right)+\frac{4}{5}\left(1-\frac{7r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)+\frac{32\pi r_{+}}{5\beta},

(109a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(109b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{12\beta r_{+}^{3}}{\pi\ell^{4}}\left(1+\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right)-\frac{24r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}},

(109c)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{6\beta r_{+}^{3}}{\pi\ell^{4}}\left(3+\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right)-\frac{24r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}.

(109d)

These results lead to the final form of
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
for the non-extremal Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
black hole embedded in the STU models, given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{5}\left(\mathfrak{c}-5\mathfrak{a}\right)-\frac{4}{5}\bigg[\left(7\mathfrak{c}+30\mathfrak{b}_{1}+30\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-8\pi\mathfrak{c}\frac{r_{+}}{\beta}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt-\frac{\beta r_{+}^{3}}{2\pi\ell^{4}}\left(\left(4\mathfrak{c}+30\mathfrak{b}_{1}+45\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)+\left(\mathfrak{c}+30\mathfrak{b}_{1}+15\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{4}}{r_{+}^{4}}\right)\bigg].

(110)

We have verified that the above relation is in perfect agreement with (
104
) for a vanishing rotation parameter, i.e.,
a=0
. Notably, there exists an additional topological term
\frac{4}{5}\mathfrak{c}
when transitioning into the non-rotating but charged background.

4.1.3
Kerr-AdS
4
black hole

In the Boyer-Lindquist coordinate system, the background metric describing the Kerr-AdS black hole is presented as a truncation of the geometry (
4.1.1
) by setting
q=0
. Specifically, the parameter
\Delta_{r}
takes a modified form,

\displaystyle\Delta_{r}=(r^{2}+a^{2})\left(1+\frac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)-2mr.

(111)

For the outer horizon at
r=r_{+}
with
\Delta_{r}(r_{+})=0
, and with the same Euclideanized setup, the mass parameter
m
and the inverse Hawking temperature
\beta
are characterized as

\displaystyle\begin{split}m&=\frac{r_{+}}{2}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),\\[5.0pt]
\beta^{-1}&=\frac{r_{+}}{4\pi\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{3r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(112)

The necessary background invariants on the KAdS
4
geometry are derived as

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
