\displaystyle=\frac{48m^{2}}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{6}}\bigg[r^{6}-15r^{2}a^{2}\left(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\cos^{2}\theta-a^{6}\cos^{6}\theta\bigg],

(113a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24}{\ell^{4}}+\frac{48m^{2}}{\left(r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)^{6}}\bigg[r^{6}-15r^{2}a^{2}\left(r^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\cos^{2}\theta-a^{6}\cos^{6}\theta\bigg],

(113b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},\qquad R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0,

(113c)

followed by their regulated integration (via holographic renormalization) relations,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=4-\frac{\beta r_{+}}{\pi\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(114a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(114b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{6\beta r_{+}}{\pi\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(114c)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0.

(114d)

All this provides the following simplified
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
relation for the non-extremal Kerr-AdS
4
black hole in the STU models

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right)-\frac{\left(\mathfrak{c}-6\mathfrak{b}_{1}\right)\beta r_{+}}{\pi\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right).

(115)

It can be verified that the above result matches the expression (
104
) by managing all the Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
terms
W_{i}
,
R_{i}
, and
F_{i}
for a vanishing
q=0
.

4.1.4
Schwarzschild-AdS
4
black hole

We derive the background metric for a Schwarzschild-AdS
4
(SchAdS
4
) black hole as a charged-truncation (i.e.,
q\to 0
) of the RNAdS
4
geometry (
105
). In this context, the corresponding background metric function
f(r)
captures a specific form,

\displaystyle f(r)=1-\frac{2m}{r}+\frac{r^{2}}{\ell^{2}}.

(116)

The inverse Hawking temperature parameter for the SchAdS
4
black hole is given by

\displaystyle\beta^{-1}=\frac{1}{4\pi r_{+}}\left(1+\frac{3r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(117)

where
r_{+}
is the horizon radius such that
f(r_{+})=0
, and it relates the mass parameter
m
via,

\displaystyle m=\frac{r_{+}}{2}\left(1+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right).

(118)

The background invariants are computed as

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{48m^{2}}{r^{6}},

(119a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24}{\ell^{4}}+\frac{48m^{2}}{r^{6}},

(119b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},\quad R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0.

(119c)

To derive the regulated values of the above integrated invariants, we proceed with the same holographic renormalization procedure detailed in
section

4.1.1
. The results are

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=4-\frac{4r_{+}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(120a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(120b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{24r_{+}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(120c)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int\mathrm{d}^{4}x\sqrt{\det\bar{g}}\,R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0.

(120d)

Finally, we find the relation to obtain the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
for the non-extremal SchAdS
4
black hole in the STU models,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Sch-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right)-\frac{4\left(\mathfrak{c}-6\mathfrak{b}_{1}\right)r_{+}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right).

(121)

The expression is found to match the results (
102
), (
4.1.2
), and (
115
) by appropriately setting
q=0
and
a=0
.

4.1.5
Extremal limits and near-horizon geometry

Now, let us delve into the extremal limit of black holes, denoted by
\beta\to\infty
, wherein the Hawking temperatures vanish, implying
T_{\text{bh}}=\beta^{-1}=0
. Applying the extremal limit to the aforementioned non-extremal backgrounds and their setups might, naively, lead to divergences. However, this challenge is effectively addressed by employing the quantum entropy function (QEF) formalism
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
, succinctly outlined in
section

2.2
. The analysis is specifically confined to the “finite” part of the extremal near-horizon (ENH) geometry, which includes an AdS
2
component.

At this stage of our work, our objective is to derive the invariants
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
,
R^{2}
, and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
(as outlined in the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formula (
4
)), along with their regulated integration results around the Euclideanized extremal near-horizon (ENH) geometry of the Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
, Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
, and Kerr-AdS
4
black holes. Notably, the extremal Schwarzschild background is not a valid geometry.
12
12

12

As an illustration, setting the Schwarzschild limit, i.e.,
a=0
and
q=0
in the relation (
136
) yields a vanishing extremal horizon
r_{0}
.
The specific process is detailed as follows.

To structure the extremal near-horizon geometry of the KNAdS
4
metric (
4.1.1
), we introduce a new parameter
\lambda
and new coordinates
\tilde{t}
,
\tilde{r}
, and
\tilde{\phi}
as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}r=r_{0}+\lambda\tilde{r},\quad t=\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{\lambda}\tilde{t},\quad\phi=\tilde{\phi}+\frac{a\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)}t,\end{gathered}

Here,
r_{0}
represents the location of the extremal horizon, satisfying
\beta^{-1}(r_{0})=0
, imposing the following extremality bounds on the KNAdS
4
black hole parameters,

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}a^{2}+q^{2}=r_{0}^{2}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{3r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),\\
m=r_{0}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{2r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right).\end{gathered}

The parameter
\ell_{2}
will be later identified as the radius of AdS
2
space characterizing the ENH AdS
4
geometry of the KNAdS
4
black hole and can be expressed as

\displaystyle\ell_{2}=\ell\sqrt{\frac{a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{a^{2}+\ell^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}}}=\ell\sqrt{\frac{2r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{2}\left(2r_{0}^{2}-q^{2}\right)}{\ell^{4}+\ell^{2}\left(6r_{0}^{2}-q^{2}\right)-3r_{0}^{4}}}.

(125)

Notably, the condition
\lambda\to 0
in transformations (
4.1.5
) represents a combined limit of the near-horizon and extremality, while keeping
\tilde{r}
,
\tilde{t}
, and
\tilde{\phi}
fixed. In this limit, along with the Euclidean continuation
\tilde{t}\to-i\tilde{\tau}
, the near-horizon coordinate transformations (
4.1.5
) restructure the KNAdS
4
metric (
4.1.1
) into the forms,

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\mu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}\rho_{0}^{2}}{a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}\left(\tilde{r}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\tau}^{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}^{2}}{\tilde{r}^{2}}\right)+\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi^{2}\rho_{0}^{2}}\left(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}+\frac{2\Xi ar_{0}\ell_{2}^{2}}{\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}i\tilde{r}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\tau}\right)^{2},

(126a)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\theta}=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad\rho_{0}^{2}=r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,\quad\Xi=1-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}.

(126b)

Similarly, one obtains the background Maxwell field (
90
) supplying the charges to the above extremal near-horizon geometry as

\displaystyle\tilde{A}=-\frac{q}{\rho_{0}^{2}}\left[\frac{i\ell_{2}^{2}}{a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}\left(r_{0}^{2}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\tilde{r}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\tau}-\frac{ar_{0}\sin^{2}\theta}{\Xi}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}\right].

(127)

From here, the Euclidean ENH metric (
126
) can be structured into a wrapped version of AdS
2
fibered over a compact space
K
, as expressed by (for instance, refer to
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
):

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\mu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\nu}=\ell_{2}^{2}f(y)\left[\left(\tilde{r}^{2}-1\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{\tau}^{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}^{2}}{\left(\tilde{r}^{2}-1\right)}\right]+\mathrm{d}s^{2}_{K}.

(128)

Here,
\left(\tilde{r},\thinspace\tilde{\tau}\right)
represent coordinates describing AdS
2
within the range
1\leq\tilde{r}<\infty
and
\tilde{\tau}\equiv 0+2\pi
. Additionally,
f(y)
is a function of coordinates
(\theta,\thinspace\tilde{\phi})
along the
K
space, collectively denoted by
y
. Importantly, the structure of
\mathrm{d}s^{2}_{K}
involves the remaining differentials that are invariant under the
SL(2,R)
isometry of AdS
2
. It follows that,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\det\tilde{g}}=G(y)=\frac{\ell^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}{(\ell^{2}-a^{2})}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\sin\theta,

(129)

where
G(y)
is a function that has no dependence on the AdS
2
coordinates.

Now, our task is to evaluate the contribution of
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
for extremal black holes by integrating the
a_{4}(x)
coefficient or the related background invariants over the ENH geometry, as structured in
eqs.

126
and
128
. However, these integrations suffer from divergences due to the infinite volume of the AdS
2
piece. As per the QEF prescription discussed in
section

2.2
, we must ignore these divergences and extract only the divergent-insensitive finite part of the ENH geometry. This finite part encodes the quantum degrees of freedom for the extremal black holes and serves as the exact result for the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution. Importantly, we do not need to employ any specific regularization scheme, such as the holographic renormalization considered for the non-extremal case in
section

4.1.1
. As a way forward, we introduce an infrared cut-off at
\tilde{r}=\tilde{r}_{c}
and expand the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formula in terms of the
a_{4}(x)
coefficient as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}

\displaystyle=\int_{1}^{\tilde{r}_{c}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\tau}\int\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\tilde{\phi}\sqrt{\det\tilde{g}}\,a_{4}(x)

\displaystyle=2\pi\left(\tilde{r}_{c}-1\right)\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)a_{4}(y).

(130)

The term proportional to
\tilde{r}_{c}
is interpreted as an infinite shift in the ground state energy in the extremal limit
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
. On the other hand, the cut-off independent finite piece is the true and unambiguous contribution to the one-loop correction for the quantum horizon degeneracy of extremal black holes. Thus, we need to discard the divergent part and identify the finite term, involving a
-2\pi
prefactor, as the desired
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution for extremal black holes. This yields the following revised formula:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}=\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)\left[\mathfrak{c}W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-\mathfrak{a}E_{4}+\mathfrak{b}_{1}R^{2}+\mathfrak{b}_{2}R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}\right],

(131)

where we have utilized the trace anomaly relation (
84
) for
a_{4}
and the integration range is
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
and
0\leq\tilde{\phi}\leq 2\pi
for the
y
coordinates on the ENH geometry (
126
). The final form of the background invariants, as well as their integrations over specific parts of the ENH geometry, are derived and separately listed in
appendix

B
. This results in the following expression for
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
for the extremal Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
black hole in STU models:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(ext,KN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=-4\mathfrak{a}-\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{2a\Xi\ell^{4}r_{0}^{5}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\bigg[3a^{7}r_{0}^{5}\left((\mathfrak{c}+16\mathfrak{b}_{1})-\frac{2\mathfrak{c}\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{4}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+a^{5}r_{0}^{7}\left(\left(39\mathfrak{c}+240\mathfrak{b}_{1}-24\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)-\left(22\mathfrak{c}-24\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{4}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+a^{3}r_{0}^{9}\left(\left(49\mathfrak{c}+336\mathfrak{b}_{1}-48\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)-\left(50\mathfrak{c}+48\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{15\mathfrak{c}\ell^{4}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+3ar_{0}^{11}\left(\left(7\mathfrak{c}+48\mathfrak{b}_{1}+24\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)-\left(6\mathfrak{c}-8\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\mathfrak{c}\ell^{4}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+3\mathfrak{c}r_{0}^{12}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}\right)^{2}\left(3+\frac{\ell^{2}+a^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{0}}\right)\bigg].

(132)

Next, we compute the same integrated invariants on the near-horizon background of extremal Kerr-AdS
4
and Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
black holes in STU models (refer to
appendix

B
). The corresponding regulated and finite contributions to
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
are presented as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right)-\frac{2\left(\mathfrak{c}-6\mathfrak{b}_{1}\right)\ell_{2}^{2}}{\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}+3r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),

(133)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(ext,RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{4}{3}\mathfrak{c}-4\mathfrak{a}+4\mathfrak{b}_{1}\right)-\left(\frac{2}{3}\mathfrak{c}+2\mathfrak{b}_{1}-\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\left(\frac{2}{3}\mathfrak{c}+2\mathfrak{b}_{1}+\mathfrak{b}_{2}\right)\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{\ell_{2}^{2}}.

(134)

Note that with appropriate limits, particularly
q=0
and
a=0
, one can successively reproduce the above formulas as consistent truncations of the Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
relation (
4.1.5
).

To ensure the consistency of our setup, we have also taken an alternative approach to obtain the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions by directly considering the extremal limit,
\beta\to\infty
, on the finite temperature backgrounds discussed in
sections

4.1.1
,
4.1.2
and
4.1.3
. This method enables us to circumvent naive divergences associated with taking the
\beta\to\infty
limit. In the initial steps, we treat the outer horizon
r_{+}
as an explicit function of the inverse temperature
\beta
, while keeping all other parameters and charges fixed. By employing a low-temperature expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle r_{+}=r_{0}+\frac{2\pi\ell_{2}^{2}}{\beta}+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{-2}\right).

(135)

Here,
\ell_{2}
represents the radius of AdS
2
space that naturally emerges in the structure of extremal black holes. On the other hand, the extremal horizon
r_{0}
is interpreted as a finite component within the non-extremal horizon radius
r_{+}
as
\beta\to\infty
. For a generic extremal Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
black hole,
\ell_{2}
is given in
eq.

125
, while the typical form of
r_{0}
is expressed as

\displaystyle r_{0}^{2}=\frac{\ell^{2}}{6}\left(\sqrt{1+\frac{2}{\ell^{2}}\left(6q^{2}+7a^{2}\right)}-\frac{a^{2}}{\ell^{2}}-1\right).

(136)

Continuing with the expansion (
135
), we can expand the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution as

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}=\mathcal{C}_{1}\beta+\mathcal{C}_{0}+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{-1}\right).

(137)

In the extremal limit as
\beta\to\infty
, the terms with inverse powers of
\beta
vanish, while the first term, linear in
\beta
, diverges. This divergence can be interpreted as an infinite shift in the ground state energy due to one-loop fluctuations. Therefore, we can safely disregard this linear and divergent term and consider the finite constant term
\mathcal{C}_{0}
as an unambiguous and effective value for the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution of extremal black holes. The results obtained by following this procedure align precisely with those derived in
sections

4.1.5
,
133
and
134
via the QEF formalism.

4.1.6
Asymptotically-flat limits

We will now explore the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions for the asymptotically flat counterpart of the previously discussed non-extremal and extremal AdS
4
black holes embedded in STU supergravity models with a vanishing cosmological constant, i.e.,
\Lambda=0
. The flat-space limit is achieved by setting
\ell\to\infty
in the relations given in
eqs.

104
,
4.1.2
,
115
,
121
,
4.1.5
,
133
and
134
. In this scenario, the outer-horizon and inverse Hawking-temperature for the non-extremal Kerr-Newman black hole are given by,

\displaystyle r_{+}=m+\sqrt{m^{2}-q^{2}-a^{2}},\quad\beta=\frac{4\pi r_{+}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}{\left(r_{+}^{2}-q^{2}-a^{2}\right)},

(138)

while
r_{0}
and
\ell_{2}
, as well as the revised Kerr parameter
b=\frac{a}{q}
controlling the related extremality, are constrained as

\displaystyle r_{0}=\sqrt{q^{2}+a^{2}},\quad\ell_{2}=\sqrt{q^{2}+2a^{2}},\quad b=\frac{r_{0}}{q}\sqrt{\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-1}.

(139)

Following that, the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas for the non-extremal and extremal Kerr-Newman black holes embedded in STU models are expressed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(KN)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right)+\frac{\mathfrak{c}\beta q^{4}}{16\pi}\bigg[\frac{\left(3a^{4}+2a^{2}r_{+}^{2}+3r_{+}^{4}\right)}{a^{4}r_{+}^{3}(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2})}+\frac{3(a^{2}-r_{+}^{2})(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2})}{a^{5}r_{+}^{4}}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg],

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(ext,KN)}}

\displaystyle=-4\mathfrak{a}+\frac{\mathfrak{c}}{2}\bigg[\frac{\left(3+24b^{2}+40b^{4}+16b^{6}\right)}{(b^{2}+1)^{2}(2b^{2}+1)}-\frac{3(2b^{2}+1)}{b{\left(b^{2}+1\right)^{5/2}}}\arctan\left(\frac{b}{\sqrt{b^{2}+1}}\right)\bigg].

(140)

Similarly, we successively derive the local contributions of non-extremal and extremal Schwarzschild (
q=0
,
a=0
), Reissner-Nordström (
a=0
,
\ell_{2}=r_{0}
, and
b=0
) and Kerr (
q=0
,
\ell_{2}=\sqrt{2}r_{0}
, and
b\to\infty
) black holes as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Sch)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right),

(141)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(Kerr)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right),\quad\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr)}}=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right),

(142)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(RN)}}

\displaystyle=4\left(\mathfrak{c}-\mathfrak{a}\right)+\frac{2\mathfrak{c}\beta q^{4}}{5\pi r_{+}^{5}},\quad\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}^{\text{(ext,RN-AdS)}}=-4\mathfrak{a}.

(143)

We have verified that the above
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
expressions are consistent with the results obtained in
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Sen:2013ns
;
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Banerjee:2020wbr
;
Karan:2020sk
;
Karan:2021teq
for asymptotically-flat
4
black holes embedded in Einstein-Maxwell (super)gravity theories.

4.2
Results

This section provides a comprehensive overview of the final outcomes of our research, specifically focusing on the logarithmic corrections to black hole entropy within two distinct scenarios of STU supergravity intersecting with
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD theories in four-dimensional spacetime (as detailed in
section

3
). Our analysis is grounded in the central formula outlined in
eq.

25
. The local component of the logarithmic corrections is derived from the corresponding
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas, as detailed in
section

4.1
, while considering trace anomaly data from (
84
) for the two STU truncations:
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)
and
(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})
. In contrast, the zero-mode contributions for various black hole backgrounds are computed using the global relation (
25c
). For the specific black holes considered in this study, we refer to the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{zm}}
data listed in
table

1
. We will further elaborate on the typical nature and significance of these logarithmic correction results in
section

5
.

4.2.1
Quantum corrected AdS
4
black holes

Case I:
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)

For the STU supergravity model truncated into a
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD-AdS theory with dilaton couplings
\kappa_{1}=1,\kappa_{2}=-1
, the logarithmic entropy correction results for Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
, Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
, Kerr-AdS
4
, and Schwarzschild-AdS
4
black holes are calculated as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Sch-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{37}{45}-\frac{343r_{+}^{2}}{20\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(144)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{82}{45}-\frac{343\beta r_{+}}{80\pi\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(145)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{562}{225}+\frac{719}{100}\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{166\pi r_{+}}{25\beta}-\frac{\beta r_{+}^{3}}{25\pi\ell^{4}}\left(121+\frac{1217\ell^{2}}{8r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{83\ell^{4}}{8r_{+}^{4}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(146)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(KN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{419}{180}+\frac{a^{4}}{32\Xi r_{+}^{2}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}\bigg\{\frac{249}{5}+\frac{1831r_{+}^{2}}{5\ell^{2}}+\frac{2r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}\left({83}+\frac{1831r_{+}^{2}}{5\ell^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{8r_{+}^{4}}{5a^{4}}\left(83+\frac{271r_{+}^{2}}{2\ell^{2}}\right)-\frac{166r_{+}^{6}}{5a^{6}}\left(1+\frac{9r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)+\frac{\beta}{40\pi r_{+}}\bigg({249}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{16r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}\left(\frac{83}{4}+\frac{769r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{2329r_{+}^{4}}{4\ell^{4}}\right)+\frac{20r_{+}^{4}}{a^{4}}\left(\frac{83}{10}-\frac{2661r_{+}^{2}}{5\ell^{2}}-\frac{749r_{+}^{4}}{2\ell^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{332r_{+}^{6}}{a^{6}}\left(1-\frac{9r_{+}^{4}}{\ell^{4}}\right)\bigg)+\frac{166\pi r_{+}}{5\beta}\left(3+\frac{8r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}+\frac{10r_{+}^{4}}{a^{4}}+\frac{8r_{+}^{6}}{a^{6}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{249\beta r_{+}^{7}}{40\pi a^{8}}\bigg(1-\frac{4\pi r_{+}}{\beta}+\frac{3r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{249\beta r_{+}^{2}}{40\pi a^{3}}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{\left(a^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}}-\frac{4\pi r_{+}}{\beta}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right)\bigg)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(147)

Similarly, in the extremal limit and near-horizon analysis (see
section

4.1.5
) for the above backgrounds, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{29}{90}-\frac{343\ell_{2}^{2}}{40\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}+3r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(148)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{101}{18}+\frac{47}{120}\left(\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{\ell_{2}^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(149)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,KN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{689}{180}+\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{80\Xi a\ell^{4}r_{0}^{5}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\right)^{2}}\bigg\{{3a^{7}r_{0}^{5}}\left(\frac{1831}{6}+\frac{83\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{83\ell^{4}}{2r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+{a^{5}r_{0}^{7}}\left(\frac{7163}{2}+\frac{913\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{83\ell^{4}}{2r_{0}^{4}}\right)+{5a^{3}r_{0}^{9}}\left(\frac{10493}{10}+\frac{415\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{249\ell^{4}}{2r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+{3ar_{0}^{11}}\left(\frac{1499}{2}+\frac{249\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{83\ell^{4}}{2r_{0}^{4}}\right)-\frac{249r_{0}^{12}}{2}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\left(3+\frac{\ell^{2}+a^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{0}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(150)

Case II:
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})

For the STU supergravity model intersecting with a
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD-AdS theory, with dilaton couplings
\kappa_{1}=\sqrt{3}
and
\kappa_{2}=-1/\sqrt{3}
, the logarithmic correction to the entropy of Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
, Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
, Kerr-AdS
4
, and Schwarzschild-AdS
4
black holes are computed as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Sch-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{37}{45}-\frac{343r_{+}^{2}}{20\left(\ell^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(151)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{82}{45}-\frac{343\beta r_{+}}{80\pi\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}+r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(152)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{586}{225}+\frac{607}{100}\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}+\frac{514\pi r_{+}}{75\beta}-\frac{\beta r_{+}^{3}}{25\pi\ell^{4}}\left(103+\frac{1161\ell^{2}}{8r_{+}^{2}}-\frac{257\ell^{4}}{24r_{+}^{4}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(153)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(KN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{443}{180}+\frac{a^{4}}{32\Xi r_{+}^{2}\left(r_{+}^{2}+a^{2}\right)}\bigg\{\frac{257}{5}+\frac{1081r_{+}^{2}}{3\ell^{2}}+\frac{2r_{+}^{2}}{3a^{2}}\left({257}+\frac{5213r_{+}^{2}}{5\ell^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{8r_{+}^{4}}{15a^{4}}\left({257}+\frac{677r_{+}^{2}}{2\ell^{2}}\right)-\frac{514r_{+}^{6}}{15a^{6}}\left(1+\frac{9r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)+\frac{\beta}{40\pi r_{+}}\bigg({257}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{80r_{+}^{2}}{3a^{2}}\left(\frac{257}{20}+\frac{2363r_{+}^{2}}{5\ell^{2}}+\frac{1351r_{+}^{4}}{4\ell^{4}}\right)+\frac{20r_{+}^{4}}{a^{4}}\left(\frac{257}{30}-\frac{7783r_{+}^{2}}{15\ell^{2}}-\frac{661r_{+}^{4}}{2\ell^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1028r_{+}^{6}}{3a^{6}}\left(1-\frac{9r_{+}^{4}}{\ell^{4}}\right)\bigg)+\frac{514\pi r_{+}}{15\beta}\left(3+\frac{8r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}+\frac{10r_{+}^{4}}{a^{4}}+\frac{8r_{+}^{6}}{a^{6}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{257\beta r_{+}^{7}}{40\pi a^{8}}\bigg(1-\frac{4\pi r_{+}}{\beta}+\frac{3r_{+}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\bigg)^{2}+\frac{257\beta r_{+}^{2}}{40\pi a^{3}}\left(1-\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{a^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}+\frac{\left(a^{2}+3r_{+}^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}}-\frac{4\pi r_{+}}{\beta}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}\right)\bigg)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(154)

On the other hand, considering the near-horizon limit in the extremal case for the above backgrounds yields

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{29}{90}-\frac{343\ell_{2}^{2}}{40\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}\left(1-\frac{a^{2}+3r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right)\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(155)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,RN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{511}{90}+\frac{47}{120}\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{13}{40}\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{\ell_{2}^{2}}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(156)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,KN-AdS)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{713}{180}+\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{16\Xi a\ell^{4}r_{0}^{5}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\right)^{2}}\bigg\{{a^{7}r_{0}^{5}}\left(\frac{1081}{6}+\frac{257\ell^{2}}{5r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{257\ell^{4}}{10r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{a^{5}r_{0}^{7}}{15}\left(\frac{21049}{2}+\frac{2731\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{257\ell^{4}}{2r_{0}^{4}}\right)+{a^{3}r_{0}^{9}}\left(\frac{10341}{10}+\frac{6617\ell^{2}}{15r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{257\ell^{4}}{2r_{0}^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle+{3ar_{0}^{11}}\left(\frac{279}{2}+\frac{739\ell^{2}}{15r_{0}^{2}}+\frac{257\ell^{4}}{30r_{0}^{4}}\right)-\frac{257r_{0}^{12}}{10}\left(1+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\left(3+\frac{\ell^{2}+a^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-\frac{a^{2}\ell^{2}}{r_{0}^{4}}\right)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{0}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(157)

All the results presented above are entirely non-topological as they are intricate functions of the respective black hole parameters and dimensionless ratios, denoted as
\left\{a,r_{+},r_{0},\ell,\ell_{2},\beta\right\}
. These parameters are distinct for each background, representing direct functions of the black hole mass, charge, and angular momentum (as detailed in
section

4.1
). Notably, the formulas derived for uncharged Kerr-AdS
4
and Schwarzschild-AdS
4
black holes in both extremal and non-extremal limits are dilaton coupling independent and identical. These quantum entropy corrections are novel and represent a significant achievement in this work.

4.2.2
Quantum corrected flat
4
black holes

Case I:
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)

For the STU supergravity model truncated into a
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD theory with dilaton couplings
\kappa_{1}=1,\kappa_{2}=-1
, the logarithmic entropy correction results for asymptotically-flat Kerr-Newman, Reissner-Nordström, Kerr and Schwarzschild black holes are calculated as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Sch)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{37}{45}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(158)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Kerr)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{82}{45}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},\quad\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr)}}=\frac{29}{90}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(159)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(RN)}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{37}{45}+\frac{83\beta q^{4}}{200\pi r_{+}^{5}}\right]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},\quad\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,RN)}}=-\frac{869}{180}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(160)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(KN)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{82}{45}+\frac{83\beta q^{4}}{1280\pi}\bigg\{\frac{\left(3a^{4}+2a^{2}r_{+}^{2}+3r_{+}^{4}\right)}{a^{4}r_{+}^{3}(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{3(a^{2}-r_{+}^{2})(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2})}{a^{5}r_{+}^{4}}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(161)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,KN)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{689}{180}+\frac{83}{160}\bigg\{\frac{\left(3+24b^{2}+40b^{4}+16b^{6}\right)}{(b^{2}+1)^{2}(2b^{2}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3(2b^{2}+1)}{b{\left(b^{2}+1\right)^{5/2}}}\arctan\left(\frac{b}{\sqrt{b^{2}+1}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(162)

Case II:
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})

For the STU supergravity model intersecting with a
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD theory, with dilaton couplings
\kappa_{1}=\sqrt{3}
and
\kappa_{2}=-1/\sqrt{3}
, the logarithmic correction to the entropy of Kerr-Newman, Reissner-Nordström, Kerr, and Schwarzschild black holes are computed as

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Sch)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{37}{45}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(163)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(Kerr)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{82}{45}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},\quad\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,Kerr)}}=\frac{29}{90}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(164)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(RN)}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{37}{45}+\frac{257\beta q^{4}}{600\pi r_{+}^{5}}\right]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},\quad\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,RN)}}=-\frac{893}{180}\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(165)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(KN)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{82}{45}+\frac{257\beta q^{4}}{3840\pi}\bigg\{\frac{\left(3a^{4}+2a^{2}r_{+}^{2}+3r_{+}^{4}\right)}{a^{4}r_{+}^{3}(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{3(a^{2}-r_{+}^{2})(a^{2}+r_{+}^{2})}{a^{5}r_{+}^{4}}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{+}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H},

(166)

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\text{BH}}^{\text{(ext,KN)}}

\displaystyle=\bigg[-\frac{713}{180}+\frac{257}{480}\bigg\{\frac{\left(3+24b^{2}+40b^{4}+16b^{6}\right)}{(b^{2}+1)^{2}(2b^{2}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3(2b^{2}+1)}{b{\left(b^{2}+1\right)^{5/2}}}\arctan\left(\frac{b}{\sqrt{b^{2}+1}}\right)\bigg\}\bigg]\ln\mathcal{A}_{H}.

(167)

In contrast to the AdS
4
results, the logarithmic correction formulas obtained for the flat
4
backgrounds are less complicated. Interestingly, their non-topological nature, which was unavoidable for the AdS
4
cases, exhibits a specific pattern for the asymptotically-flat backgrounds. For charged backgrounds, the results remain non-topological but are considerably more streamlined in terms of background parameters and dimensionless ratios, as illustrated in
section

4.1.6
. However, in the extremal limit, the Reissner-Nordström formulas become expressions in terms of numerical ratios, completely independent of geometric parameters. Similarly, in the limit of vanishing charge (
q=0,b\to\infty
), the logarithmic correction relations for Kerr and Schwarzschild black holes become entirely topological, both in the extremal and non-extremal limits. Readers familiar with the literature may recognize this characteristic pattern, as observed in previous works such as
Karan:2021teq
;
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Sen:2013ns
. Additionally, the Kerr-Newman results are always found to be transcendental instead of rational. Further discussion of the non-topological versus topological logarithmic correction results as well as their implications are presented in the next section.

5
Summary and discussions

In this paper, we computed the logarithmic corrections to the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of black holes in 4D STU supergravity
Cremmer:1984hj
;
Duff:1995sm
. Specifically, this study considers the scenario where STU models are viewed as a
U(1)^{4}
-charged EMD theory
Cvetic:1999xp
;
Clement:2013fc
;
Chow:2014cca
;
Cvetic:2014vsa
;
Cvetic:2021lss
;
Anabalon:2022aig
and further truncated into two specific
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD systems
Clement:2013fc
;
Chow:2014cca
;
Cvetic:2014vsa
;
Lu:2013eoa
. These systems are characterized by one dilaton coupled non-minimally to two Maxwell fields, with the specific coupling constant values
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)
and
(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})
in the action (
37
). Subsequently, we demonstrated how setting appropriate constraints on the two
U(1)
Maxwell backgrounds/charges, i.e.,
{Q_{2}}={\kappa_{1}}{Q_{1}}=-\frac{{Q_{1}}}{{\kappa_{2}}}
, uplifts the entire Kerr-Newman-AdS family of black holes and their flat counterparts, embedding them within the two cases of truncated STU systems. This technically defines our choice of EM embedding, where the Einstein-Maxwell backgrounds (both cosmological and flat) non-trivially satisfy the
U(1)^{2}
-charged STU equations of motion for a null dilaton background. In the future, we aspire to surmount the obstacles associated with going beyond the charge-constrained or EM-embedding limit and explore the quantum black hole entropy within STU models characterized by a non-vanishing dilaton background.

Notably, one should not be concerned about the absence of any fermionic contribution in the main results presented in
sections

3.4
and
4.2
. The current STU models are essentially a bosonic truncation of
\mathcal{N}=8
supergravity, which includes multiple gauge and scalar moduli fields
Cremmer:1978ds
;
Cremmer:1979up
. As discussed, these models have undergone further truncations into
U(1)^{4}
and
U(1)^{2}
-charged EMD systems (
36
) and (
37
), forming the central or exact bosonic sector of all
\mathcal{N}\geq 2
supergravity theories and providing them with general black hole solutions
Cvetic:1996zq
. In essence, STU models are of particular interest as they serve as universal bosonic building blocks within supergravity/low-energy string vacua. However, one can utilize the current STU results at any point and explore the full supergravity theories (minimal as well as matter-coupled), where the
a_{4}(x)
and logarithmic correction relations receive contributions from additional one-loop fermionic and bosonic fluctuations in supergravity, vector, and other matter multiplets. When considering the embedding of the same black holes, it is essential to remember that the additional fermionic and bosonic content must have a vanishing background but fluctuate around the common EM backgrounds (for details, see
Banerjee:2011pp
;
Sen:2012qq
;
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Banerjee:2020wbr
;
Karan:2020sk
;
David:2021eoq
).

The logarithmic correction relations for the embedded AdS
4
and flat
4
black holes, as detailed in
eqs.

144
,
145
,
146
,
147
,
148
,
149
,
150
,
158
,
159
,
160
,
161
,
162
,
151
,
152
,
153
,
154
,
155
,
156
,
157
,
163
,
164
,
165
,
166
and
167
, are computed using Euclidean quantum gravity setups
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
;
Sen:2013ns
as elaborated in
section

2
, followed by the heat kernel method-based Seeley-DeWitt expansion
Vassilevich:2003ll
. In the local part, the four dimensional
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions depend only on the
a_{4}(x)
coefficient. The relevant data (
3.4
) is obtained by fluctuating the STU content around the embedded EM backgrounds and is re-expressed in terms of appropriate geometric invariants and conformal anomalies in
eq.

84
. The related central charges and anomaly coefficients are determined in terms of generic formulas (
84b
) involving dilaton couplings
\kappa_{1}
or
\kappa_{2}
. In special limiting cases, the obtained heat kernel data aligns perfectly with previously available results for the Einstein-Maxwell theory
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Karan:2021teq
;
Sen:2013ns
, or the bosonic sector of
\mathcal{N}=2
supergravity (
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=0
)
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Karan:2020sk
;
David:2021eoq
;
Sen:2012qq
;
Keeler:2014nn
, the Kaluza-Klein system (
\kappa_{1}=\sqrt{3},\kappa_{2}=0,\Lambda=0
)
Castro:2018tg
, and Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton models (
\kappa_{1}=1,\sqrt{3},1/\sqrt{3}
and
\kappa_{2}=0
)
Karan:2022dfy
. Furthermore, we independently verified the computed
a_{4}(x)
relation through manual calculation and by developing
Mathematica
algorithms using xAct
Garcia:2002wp
and xPert
Brizuela:2008ra
. These validations enhance our confidence in the consistency of
a_{4}(x)
derivations and logarithmic entropy correction results achieved in this work.

All the results presented in
sections

4.2.1
and
4.2.2
are obtained by integrating the Weyl anomaly
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
, the Euler density
E_{4}
, and other background invariants such as
R^{2}
and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
, which constitute the central
a_{4}(x)
relation (
84
). Specifically, these integrated invariant relations, along with their central charge coefficients
(\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}_{1},\mathfrak{b}_{2})
, collectively evaluate the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
formulas outlined in
section

4.1
. For all AdS
4
black holes, this integration process necessitates the inclusion of a holographic boundary term (
97
) to regulate the divergences arising from their infinite volume. This choice of regularization naturally aligns with the Gauss-Bonnet-Chern theorem
Chern:1945wp
, resulting in a consistent and unambiguous result by isolating the finite portion from the diverging bulk one-loop effective action. The holographic renormalization choice yields the consistent 4D Euler characteristic value of
\chi=2
when integrating
E_{4}
around all AdS
4
backgrounds of interest. Furthermore, we have verified that the integrated AdS
4
invariants precisely match the established relations in
Sen:2013ns
;
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2020sk
;
Karan:2021teq
;
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
for asymptotically-flat
4
black holes in the limit
\ell\to\infty
. Around these flat backgrounds, invariants such as
R^{2}
and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
vanish, and
a_{4}(x)
is solely governed by
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and
E_{4}
anomalies, along with non-vanishing central charge values
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{a}
. In this flat-space scenario, the integration of
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
and
E_{4}
over the non-extremal backgrounds always traces out a finite volume and does not require the utilization of any regularization treatment or incorporation of boundary terms.

The regularization procedure of the integrated background invariants over all AdS
4
and flat
4
black holes in their extremal limit rely on the prescription of quantum entropy function (QEF) formalism
Sen:2008wa
;
Sen:2009wb
;
Sen:2009wc
, as detailed in
section

2.2
. Here, the underlying analysis is confined to the cut-off independent “finite” part of the extremal near-horizon (ENH) geometry, which includes an AdS
2
component and suffers divergences due to its infinite volume. Following the QEF prescription, we ignored these divergences and extracted only the divergent-insensitive finite part of the ENH geometry. This treatment serves as the exact result for the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions provided in
section

4.1.5
. Moreover, these extremal results are further verified by directly taking the
\beta\to\infty
limit on all the non-extremal backgrounds considered in
sections

4.1.1
,
4.1.2
and
4.1.3
. This ensures that the extremal logarithmic correction results presented in this paper are unambiguous and robust.

However, there are instances reported in the literature
Liu:2017vll
;
Jeon:2017ij
where the AdS
4
logarithmic corrections derived from extremal near-horizon analyses do not align with the results obtained from field theory computations. It is important to resolve this discrepancy and highlight that the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions remain consistent across all treatments—whether involving the full geometry or focusing on the near-horizon of extremal black holes. Actually, the differences arise in the zero-mode or
\mathcal{C}_{\text{zm}}
contributions, leading to distinct total logarithmic correction outcomes when employing different approaches to analyze the two facets of extremal black hole geometry. In the future, it would be intriguing to investigate whether the degrees of freedom responsible for the zero-mode quantum entropy in extremal AdS black holes reside in the near-horizon region, encompass the full geometry, or exist elsewhere. Progress in this direction may require determining the appropriate ensemble and scalings for extremal AdS black hole backgrounds.

In the current choice of STU supergravity models, we observe that all the AdS
4
logarithmic correction formulas in
eqs.

144
,
145
,
146
,
147
,
148
,
149
,
150
,
151
,
152
,
153
,
154
,
155
,
156
and
157
are guaranteed to be non-topological and represented by non-trivial functions of different black hole parameters. However, their flat-space counterparts in
eqs.

158
,
159
,
160
,
161
,
162
,
163
,
164
,
165
,
166
and
167
exhibit a relatively simplified yet contrasting nature. Specifically, in the absence of the AdS
4
boundary, a vanishing charge (as in the Kerr and Schwarzschild cases) ensures confirmed topological or black hole parameter-independent numerical results. Moreover, the extremal limit guarantees the same nature for all uncharged (e.g., Kerr) and non-rotating (e.g., Reissner-Nordström) backgrounds, while the charged-rotating Kerr-Newman result remains non-topological. Readers familiar with the literature may recognize a similar characteristic pattern of logarithmic corrections as reported in previous works
Karan:2021teq
;
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Sen:2013ns
;
Karan:2022dfy
;
David:2021eoq
.

The aforementioned observation might set a stringent criterion for four-dimensional supergravity models to admit a UV completion, which is highly sensitive to the microscopic analysis of the relevant black holes. In fact, we have already seen that the available microstate counting data
Strominger:1996sh
;
Maldacena:1996gb
;
Horowitz:1996fn
;
Emparan:2006it
;
Sen:2014aja
;
Belin:2016knb
;
Benini:2019dyp
;
Gang:2019uay
;
PandoZayas:2020iqr
;
Liu:2017vbl
;
Liu:2017vll
;
Benini:2015eyy
is indeed topological. However, in all even dimensions, quantum black holes exhibiting non-topological logarithmic corrections are a generic and natural feature (e.g., see
David:2021eoq
;
Karan:2021teq
;
Karan:2022dfy
;
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Sen:2013ns
). Notably, for a parent theory in odd-
D
dimensions, the Seeley-DeWitt coefficients
a_{D}(x)
characterizing
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
vanish due to the lack of any diffeomorphism invariant scalar functions connected to the concerned background
Vassilevich:2003ll
, and the
\Delta S_{\text{BH}}
formula is entirely defined by the topological
\mathcal{C}_{\text{zm}}
contributions. Such an expectation has also been confirmed in various 11D supergravity computations
Benini:2019dyp
;
Gang:2019uay
;
PandoZayas:2020iqr
;
Liu:2017vbl
.

In order to anticipate a topological logarithmic correction in even dimensions following the current setup of this study, one needs to either set
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{b}_{1}=\mathfrak{b}_{2}=0
or somehow constrain the integrated invariants, such as
W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
R^{2}
and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
, to have a vanishing or topological value.
13
13

13

Note that the integrated
E_{4}
always defines a topological Euler characteristic value.
Typically, these two classes of criteria, as well as their possible overlaps, are found to be fulfilled while considering the supergravity backgrounds with anomaly and other non-trivial cancellations
Charles:2015nn
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Karan:2020sk
;
Castro:2018tg
;
David:2021eoq
;
Bobev:2023dwx
, adhering to extremality conditions, approaching the flat-space limit, having vanishing charge or rotation parameters, turning off the Maxwell backgrounds, etc.
14
14

14

For example, readers are encouraged to review
Bhattacharyya:2012ss
;
Sen:2013ns
;
Charles:2015nn
;
Castro:2018tg
;
Karan:2019sk
;
Karan:2020sk
;
Karan:2021teq
;
Karan:2022dfy
;
Banerjee:2020wbr
;
Banerjee:2021pdy
, as well as
sections

4.1.6
and
B
of this paper.
However, as soon as one considers even-dimensional AdS backgrounds, the logarithmic correction formulas are guaranteed to have non-topological characteristics due to the appearance of a natural and robust boundary
\ell
(with
\ell^{2}=-12/R=-3/\Lambda
), which eventually declines or neutralize the simultaneous occurrence of available topological constraints
David:2021eoq
;
Karan:2022dfy
;
Bobev:2023dwx
. Arguably, this reasoning provides a consistent explanation for the specific nature of logarithmic correction results obtained for all Kerr-Newman-AdS and Kerr-Newman families of black holes embedded within the four-dimensional STU supergravity models.

However, discrepancies have been reported in certain cases where logarithmic corrections derived through different computational methods deviate from the expected non-topological nature. For instance, in the context of ten-dimensional massive IIA supergravity, the microscopic computations for the logarithmic entropy corrections of AdS
4
black holes appear to be topological
Liu:2018bac
. To address this puzzle, we suspect that incorporating matter multiplets induced from the full Kaluza-Klein (KK) tower modes could be crucial in higher-dimensional supergravity theories to preserve the true character of logarithmic correction relations. Similarly, in the computation of logarithmic corrections for BPS black holes in four-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=2
gauged supergravity presented in
Hristov:2021zai
, the results are reported to be topological, which contradicts the outcomes in
David:2021eoq
and our expectation. As a potential resolution, we argue that the topological characteristic in
Hristov:2021zai
explicitly emerges from the considered Euler term. However, it might be possible to recover the true non-topological nature of logarithmic corrections by incorporating the
\eta
-invariant term as a correction due to the presence of a boundary
Atiyah:1975jf
.

In conclusion, the logarithmic correction to the entropy of extremal and non-extremal AdS black holes embedded in any even-dimensional parent theory confirms a non-topological nature compared to their flat-space counterparts. This insight provides a natural and more comprehensive “infrared window into the microstates” of the black holes. We argue that the presence of AdS boundary must be interpreted as a robust and general criterion for this ‘non-topological’ characteristic, which is also sensitive to the microscopic details of the black holes. Therefore, the current study sheds light on novel aspects of black hole properties in this specific class of STU supergravity theories and significantly contributes to understanding low-energy effective string theory models in 4D. It would be intriguing to extend this topological vs. non-topological analysis to the recently explored near-extremal black holes
Iliesiu:2022onk
;
Banerjee:2023quv
;
Kapec:2023ruw
;
Rakic:2023vhv
;
Banerjee:2023gll
, even in higher-dimensional supergravity. A more challenging task would involve realizing and interpreting the non-topological nature of logarithmic corrections through progress in microstate counting within the framework of UV-completed string theory counterparts. We aim to explore some of these advancements in future research.

Appendix A
Heat kernel trace computations for
a_{4}(x)
in STU models

A.1
Notation and background identities

In this paper, we considered the two Maxwell field strengths, denoted as
\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{1}{\mu\nu}}}
and
\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{2}{\mu\nu}}}
, which play a crucial role in embedding EM backgrounds into the
U(1)^{2}
-charged STU supergravity models for two sets of coupling constants:
(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})=(1,-1)
and
(\sqrt{3},-1/\sqrt{3})
. They lead to solely electrically charged configurations that satisfy either
Q_{2}=\kappa_{1}Q_{1}
or
Q_{1}=\kappa_{2}Q_{2}
(for detailed discussions, see
sections

3.1
and
3.2
). Throughout our analysis, we maintained strict control over the Maxwell field strengths by imposing a set of constraint relations:

\displaystyle R_{\mu\rho\nu\sigma}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\sigma}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}

In deriving the above identities, we have considered the gravitational Bianchi identities and related relations whenever needed:

\displaystyle R_{\mu[\nu\rho\sigma]}=0,\qquad R_{\mu\rho\nu\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}.

(179)

Notably, we have adjusted terms involving covariant derivatives on the Maxwell field strengths up to total derivatives, accompanied by the commutation relation of covariant derivatives for a rank-2 tensor, as follows (for both

A.2

and

In this section, we demonstrate the derivation of the components of matrices

and

, which are essential for proceeding with the heat kernel treatment discussed in

. To achieve this, we employ the formulas presented in

and

\mathcal{I}^{{{\tilde{\phi}_{m}}{\tilde{\phi}_{n}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.35783pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.35783pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.12862pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.12862pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.99672pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.99672pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.15639pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.15639pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}

(196)

Notice that the aforementioned relations rely on the
\mathcal{I}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.6106pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.6106pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.49263pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.65846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.24948pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.24948pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5204pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.68623pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\phi_{m}}{\phi_{n}}}}
form of the projection matrices, which are expressed as follows

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{\hat{h}\hat{h}}&=I^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tilde{\Phi}}{\tilde{\Phi}}}}=1,\quad I^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.24013pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.24013pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.949pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.20897pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.70816pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.70816pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56902pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.82898pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{1\mu}}{a_{1\nu}}}}=I^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.24013pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.24013pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.949pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.20897pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.70816pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.70816pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.56902pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.82898pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{2\mu}}{a_{2\nu}}}}=\bar{g}_{\mu\nu},\\[5.0pt]
I_{\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}\hat{h}_{\alpha\beta}}&=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\bar{g}_{\mu\alpha}\bar{g}_{\nu\beta}+\bar{g}_{\mu\beta}\bar{g}_{\nu\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{g}_{\mu\nu}\bar{g}_{\alpha\beta}\Big).\end{split}

(197)

\displaystyle\quad\kern (213)

It is important to emphasize that during the evaluation of
\left[D_{\rho},D_{\sigma}\right]
contributions for the aforementioned components, we applied the covariant derivative commutation operations, as depicted in
eqs.

31e
,
31b
and
31a
. In this process, we treated the traceless graviton
\hat{h}_{\mu\nu}
as a two-rank tensor, the Maxwell fluctuations
\left(a_{1\alpha},a_{2\alpha}\right)
as vectors, and the trace graviton
\hat{h}
and dilaton
\tilde{\Phi}
as scalars.

A.3
Traces and background invariants

In this section, we finally turn to our goal of computing the traces
\text{Tr}(E)
,
\text{Tr}(E^{2})
and
\text{Tr}\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)
for the fluctuated STU models encountered in this paper. In terms of the components of matrices
E
and
\Omega_{\rho\sigma}
, these traces can be defined as

\displaystyle\text{Tr}(E)

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{1}^{2}\left(D_{\mu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{1}{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)\left(D_{\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\mu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)-2\kappa_{1}^{2}\left(D_{\rho}\bar{F}_{1\mu\nu}\right)\left(D^{\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{1}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right),

(242)

\displaystyle{\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\right)}^{{{a_{2\alpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.07904pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.339pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\tilde{\Phi}}}}{\left(\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)}^{{{\tilde{\Phi}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.07904pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.339pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a_{2\alpha}}}}

\displaystyle={\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\right)}^{{{\tilde{\Phi}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.07904pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.339pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a_{2\alpha}}}}{\left(\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)}^{{{a_{2\alpha}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.07904pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.339pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\tilde{\Phi}}}}

\displaystyle={\left(\Omega_{\rho\sigma}\right)}^{{{a_{2\alpha}}{\tilde{\Phi}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.07904pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.339pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}{\left(\Omega^{\rho\sigma}\right)}^{{{\tilde{\Phi}}{a_{2\beta}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0592pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0592pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81978pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07974pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\mathcal{I}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.42218pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.07904pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.339pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.0592pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.0592pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81978pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07974pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a_{2\alpha}}{a_{2\beta}}}}\mathcal{I}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tilde{\Phi}}{\tilde{\Phi}}}}

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{2}^{2}\left(D_{\mu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{2}{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)\left(D_{\nu}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho\mu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right)-2\kappa_{2}^{2}\left(D_{\rho}\bar{F}_{2\mu\nu}\right)\left(D^{\rho}\bar{F}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu\nu}}}_{{{2}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\right).

(243)

Finally, we utilize all the trace data mentioned above in the definitions (
214
), (
215
), and (
216
). Subsequently, we simplify the results and background invariants with the assistance of the one-shell identities discussed in
section

A.1
. This process essentially yields the irreducible form of trace relations, as presented in
eqs.

67
,
68
and
69
, respectively.

Appendix B
Integrated background invariants in extremal near-horizon limit

This section lists the explicit forms of the background curvature invariants
W^{2}=W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
,
E_{4}
,
R^{2}
and
R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}
their integrations for the extremal near-horizon (ENH) backgrounds of Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
, Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
and Kerr-AdS
4
black holes as well as their asymptotically-flat counterparts considered in this paper. These results prove to be essential for the analysis of
sections

4.1.5
and
4.1.6
to derive the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contributions in the extremal limit of the black holes.

Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
ENH background

For the specific structure (
126
) of the ENH geometry of the Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
black hole, the necessary four-derivative invariants can be derived in terms of the independent parameters
\left\{r_{0},\ell,a\right\}
. Here, the extremality condition is imposed on
\left(r_{0},\ell_{2}\right)
or
\left(a,q\right)
via the following constraints

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}a^{2}+q^{2}=\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\left(\ell^{2}+a^{2}+3r_{0}^{2}\right),\\
\ell_{2}^{2}={\frac{\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)}{\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}+6r_{0}^{2}\right)}}=\frac{\ell^{2}\left(2r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{2}\left(2r_{0}^{2}-q^{2}\right)\right)}{\left(\ell^{4}+\ell^{2}\left(6r_{0}^{2}-q^{2}\right)-3r_{0}^{4}\right)}.\end{gathered}

Then the ENH invariants take the following forms

\displaystyle W^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{48}{\ell^{4}\rho_{0}^{12}}\bigg[r_{0}^{4}\left(r_{0}^{4}-a^{2}\ell^{2}\right)^{2}+a^{8}\ell^{4}\cos^{4}\theta-a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\Big\{a^{4}\left(a^{2}+2r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)^{2}\cos^{4}\theta

\displaystyle-a^{2}\Big(16r_{0}^{4}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+8a^{2}\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+2r_{0}^{2}\left(3a^{4}+13a^{2}\ell^{2}+3\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+9r_{0}^{6}\Big)\cos^{2}\theta+6a^{4}\ell^{4}-4r_{0}^{6}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)+8a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+r_{0}^{4}\left(a^{4}+6a^{2}\ell^{2}+\ell^{4}\right)-6r_{0}^{8}\Big\}\cos^{2}\theta\bigg],

(246a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{\ell^{4}\rho_{0}^{12}}\bigg[a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\Big(3a^{4}\left(a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta+6a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+15r_{0}^{4}\right)\cos^{4}\theta

\displaystyle+5\big\{a^{4}\left(10r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+7r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{4}\right)+2a^{2}\left(5r_{0}^{2}\ell^{4}+16r_{0}^{4}\ell^{2}+9r_{0}^{6}\right)

\displaystyle+r_{0}^{4}\left(7\ell^{4}+18r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+18r_{0}^{4}\right)\big\}-6a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\big\{a^{4}+2a^{2}\left(2r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+4r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-6r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{4}\big\}\cos^{2}\theta\Big)-2a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\Big(a^{4}\left(22r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+4r_{0}^{4}+19\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+2a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\left(11\ell^{4}+7r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-3r_{0}^{4}\right)+2r_{0}^{4}\left(2\ell^{4}-3r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-9r_{0}^{4}\right)\Big)\cos^{2}\theta

\displaystyle-r_{0}^{4}\Big(a^{4}\left(r_{0}^{4}-2r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-5\ell^{4}\right)+a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\left(8r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+6r_{0}^{4}-2\ell^{4}\right)+r_{0}^{4}\ell^{2}\left(6r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\Big)\bigg],

(246b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},

(246c)

\displaystyle R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{\ell^{4}\rho_{0}^{8}}\Big(3r_{0}^{4}+\left(a^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)r_{0}^{2}-a^{2}\ell^{2}\Big)\Big(r_{0}^{4}-6a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\Big),

(246d)

where
\rho_{0}^{2}=r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta
. Next, the integration results of above background invariants over the desired part of ENH geometry, as detailed in the formula (
131
), are successively obtained as

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\hskip-7.22743pt\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)W^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{8a^{4}\Xi\ell^{4}r_{0}^{5}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\bigg[a^{2}r_{0}\Big(9a^{8}\left(2r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-r_{0}^{4}-\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+a^{6}r_{0}^{2}\left(70r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-135r_{0}^{4}+\ell^{4}\right)+2a^{4}r_{0}^{4}\left(106r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-77r_{0}^{4}+27\ell^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+6a^{2}r_{0}^{6}\left(18r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}-13r_{0}^{4}+3\ell^{4}\right)-3r_{0}^{8}\left(2r_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}+15r_{0}^{4}-\ell^{4}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle-3r_{0}^{11}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)^{2}-12a^{3}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}\Big(a^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}-\ell^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+r_{0}^{2}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)\Big)^{2}\arctan\left(\frac{a}{r_{0}}\right)\bigg],

(247a)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(247b)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)R^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{24\ell_{2}^{2}}{\Xi\ell^{4}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)}\left(a^{4}+4a^{2}r_{0}^{2}+3r_{0}^{4}\right),

(247c)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\hskip-7.22743pt\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{12\ell_{2}^{2}\left(a^{2}-r_{0}^{2}\right)}{\Xi\ell^{4}\left(a^{2}+r_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}}\Big(r_{0}^{2}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)-a^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-r_{0}^{2}\right)\Big),

(247d)

where
\Xi=\frac{\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}}
. The above extremal near-horizon integrations over
y
coordinates must be performed in the range
0\leq\theta\leq\pi
and
0\leq\tilde{\phi}\leq 2\pi
for the following typical form of the ENH metric function,

\displaystyle G(y)=\frac{\ell^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}{(\ell^{2}-a^{2})}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\right)\sin\theta.

(248)

All the derived results (
247
) can be employed to calculate the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution for the extremal Kerr-Newman-AdS
4
black hole, as listed in
section

4.1.5
.

Kerr-AdS
4
ENH background

For a similar analysis of the Kerr-AdS
4
black hole, one needs to consider the special case
q=0
of the ENH geometry (
126
). This adjustment further revises all extremal charges and parameters, as indicated by the following bounds

\displaystyle a^{2}=\frac{r_{0}^{2}\left(3r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}{\left(\ell^{2}-r_{0}^{2}\right)},\quad\ell_{2}^{2}=\frac{2\ell^{2}r_{0}^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)}{\left(\ell^{4}+6\ell^{2}r_{0}^{2}-3r_{0}^{4}\right)},

(249)

where one must consider
r_{0}<\ell
. We can now express all the background invariants over the extremal ENH geometry of the Kerr-AdS
4
black hole in much simpler forms,

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{\ell^{4}\rho_{0}^{8}r_{0}^{2}}\left(r_{0}^{2}+a^{2}\right)^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}+\ell^{2}\right)^{2}\Big(r_{0}^{6}-a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta\left(15r_{0}^{4}-15a^{2}r_{0}^{2}\cos^{2}\theta+a^{4}\cos^{4}\theta\right)\Big),

(250a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{24}{\ell^{2}}+W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma},

(250b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{144}{\ell^{4}},\quad R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}=0.

(250c)

Subsequently, the ENH integrations of the above curvature invariants to compute the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
contribution (
133
) are derived as

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=4-\frac{2\ell_{2}^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}-3r_{0}^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)},

(251a)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(251b)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{12\ell_{2}^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}-3r_{0}^{2}\right)}{\ell^{2}\left(\ell^{2}-a^{2}\right)},

(251c)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0.

(251d)

Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
ENH background

The extremal near-horizon geometry of the electrically-charged Reissner-Nordström-AdS
4
black hole is obtained through appropriate coordinate transformations on the background metric (
105
),

\displaystyle r=r_{0}+\lambda\tilde{r},\quad t=\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{\lambda}\tilde{t}.

(252)

Upon introducing the Euclideanized time
\tilde{t}\to-i\tilde{\tau}
and taking the limit
\lambda\to 0
, the extremal near-horizon (ENH) geometry can be expressed in the desired form of AdS
{}_{2}\times S^{2}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{\mu\nu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\mu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\nu}=\ell_{2}^{2}\left[\left(\tilde{r}^{2}-1\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{\tau}^{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{r}^{2}}{\left(\tilde{r}^{2}-1\right)}\right]+r_{0}^{2}\left(\mathrm{d}\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta\mathrm{d}\phi^{2}\right),

(253)

Notably, for this non-rotating (
a=0
) charged background, the extremality bounds on the
r_{0}
and
\ell_{2}
parameters are given by,

\displaystyle q^{2}=r_{0}^{2}\left(1+\frac{3r_{0}^{2}}{\ell^{2}}\right),\quad\frac{1}{\ell_{2}^{2}}-\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}=\frac{6}{\ell^{2}},

(254)

where we must operate in the range
r_{0}>\ell_{2}
. The curvature invariants around the ENH geometry (
253
) are computed as follows

\displaystyle W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\left(r_{0}^{2}-\ell_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}}{3r_{0}^{4}\ell_{2}^{4}},

(255a)

\displaystyle E_{4}

\displaystyle=-\frac{8}{\ell_{2}r_{0}^{2}},

(255b)

\displaystyle R^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\left(r_{0}^{2}-\ell_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}}{r_{0}^{4}\ell_{2}^{4}},

(255c)

\displaystyle R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{4q^{2}\left(r_{0}^{2}-\ell_{2}\right)^{2}}{r_{0}^{6}\ell_{2}^{2}}.

(255d)

Finally, we carry out integrations of the mentioned ENH curvature invariants to derive the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
relation, as outlined in
eq.

134
. The specific results are presented as

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}-\frac{2\left(r_{0}^{4}+\ell_{2}^{4}\right)}{3r_{0}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}},

(256a)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)E_{4}

\displaystyle=4,

(256b)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)R^{2}

\displaystyle=4-\frac{2\left(r_{0}^{4}+\ell_{2}^{4}\right)}{r_{0}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}},

(256c)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)R\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}\bar{F}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(r_{0}^{4}-\ell_{2}^{4}\right)}{r_{0}^{2}\ell_{2}^{2}}.

(256d)

ENH backgrounds in flat limit (
\ell\to\infty
)

We will now investigate the flat-space limit,
\ell\to\infty
, applied to the previously derived integrated extremal near-horizon (ENH) invariants of asymptotically-AdS
4
black hole configurations. This limit results in
R=-\frac{12}{\ell^{2}}=0
, leading to similar integrated relations for the asymptotically-flat
4
black holes. In this flat-space scenario, the extremality conditions for the generic near-horizon parameters
r_{0}
and
\ell_{2}
, as well as for the revised Kerr parameter
b=\frac{a}{q}
, can be expressed as follows

\displaystyle r_{0}^{2}=q^{2}+a^{2},\quad\ell_{2}^{2}=q^{2}+2a^{2},\quad b^{2}=\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{q^{2}}\left(\frac{\ell_{2}^{2}}{r_{0}^{2}}-1\right).

(257)

For the case of a flat extremal Kerr-Newman black hole solution in STU models, the non-vanishing ENH integrated invariants are

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(b^{2}+1)^{2}(2b^{2}+1)}\bigg[\left(3+24b^{2}+40b^{4}+16b^{6}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3(2b^{2}+1)^{2}}{b\sqrt{b^{2}+1}}\arctan\left(\frac{b}{\sqrt{b^{2}+1}}\right)\bigg],

(258a)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)E_{4}

\displaystyle=4.

(258b)

In the special case of an extremal Kerr black hole (
q=0
,
\ell_{2}=\sqrt{2}r_{0}
, and
b\to\infty
), the same relations are give by

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=4,

(259a)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)E_{4}

\displaystyle=4.

(259b)

Similarly, for the asymptotically-flat extremal Reissner-Nordström black hole (
a=0
,
\ell_{2}=r_{0}
, and
b=0
) in STU models, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)W_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}W^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle=0,

(260a)

\displaystyle\frac{\left(-2\pi\right)}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{\text{ENH}}\mathrm{d}^{2}y\,G(y)E_{4}

\displaystyle=4.

(260b)

All the integrated results mentioned above are appropriately employed in deriving the
\mathcal{C}_{\text{local}}
expressed in
sections

4.1.6
,
142
and
143
.
